6 Teletext packets and pages

DOC-1783 · section

6.1 A packet on a television line

DOC-2120 · reference · informative · requirements REQ-24, REQ-443, REQ-447, REQ-448, REQ-449

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-447 Framing code: found within two bits of its expected place, one wrong bit tolerated only on lines where a packet is expected (not implemented on livedevelopment).

A receiver that reads television lines, not a stream of packets, finds each packet in the line's samples. This is what the engine's slicer does. A receiver that takes T42 packets from a file or a connection has no use for it, and nothing here binds another slicer.

The clock run-in. The slicer searches for the run-in over a configurable window, by default the whole line. The nominal position is 12.0 microseconds, minus 1.0 and plus 0.4, from the synchronising edge, but capture offsets are often unknown. It matches the alternating pattern of bits 3 to 16 and does not rely on the first two bits, which clause 6.1 warns may be reduced or absent. From the run-in it estimates the levels of 0 and 1, the threshold midway between them and the bit phase to a fraction of a sample, and holds the threshold for the rest of the line.

The framing code. After the run-in it looks for 11100100, in transmission order, within two bit periods either way of its expected place. One wrong bit is accepted only on a line where a packet is expected, one already seen to carry teletext in the same recording or stream, and only when the two packet address bytes that follow decode with no uncorrectable error. Everywhere else the code must match exactly. The slicer reports whether a correction was needed. Not built: a strict option that asks for an exact code on every line.

Bytes. It then assembles 336 bits into 42 bytes, the first bit of each byte as bit 0. Every input, from any source, becomes this one form: the 42 bytes after the framing code, each byte in transmission order with its first transmitted bit as bit 0, and parity and Hamming bits kept. All decoding works from it.

Other signals. Lines carrying the video programming signal on line 16, wide-screen signalling on line 23, test signals or scrambling data are rejected by the run-in match, the framing code and the packet address's Hamming check together.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 6.1 Clock run-in; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 6.2 Framing code; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.1 Elements of a Teletext packet; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 annex P Non-Teletext signals in the VBI

6.2 The packet

DOC-1797 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-166, REQ-1462

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1462 Only packets of the allowed kinds downstream; a receiver discards, counts and reports the rest (tested, not recorded as met on main).

Everything a receiver decodes is a sequence of 42-byte packets, bytes 4 to 45 of a broadcast packet: no clock run-in and no framing code, in transmission bit order, with parity and Hamming coding intact. This is the T42 form. A receiver MUST accept it from a file and from a WebSocket's binary messages alike.

NeoTeletext's own transports never carry the clock run-in or framing code, and the signal levels and bit rate of the standard's clauses 4 to 6 apply only to the slicer that reads recorded video.

On a live connection a valid packet has an address that decodes, and is one of a page's packets X/0 to X/28, a magazine's packet M/29, packet 8/30, or a packet of data channel 12. A server MUST send only valid packets. A receiver MUST discard and count anything else, without closing the connection.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.1 Elements of a Teletext packet; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.1 Overview of packet types; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 1 Summary of packet types, their function and application; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 8.2 Hamming 8/4

6.3 Byte coding and error protection

DOC-1798 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-114, REQ-115, REQ-116, REQ-117, REQ-118, REQ-119, REQ-357

Verified by inspection. REQ-357 Bits and bytes that the standard marks as reserved are ignored when decoding: Read at engine 6112177: reserved data has no effect in `packet/editorial_links.rs` (only data bit 4 of the link control byte), `packet/triplet_fields.rs` (D3 of Display Attributes), `packet/function.rs` and `level15.rs` (data below 2/0). No test can cover this, because the requirement is universal over every reserved bit of the standard, and `every_mode_has_its_fields` and `magazine_packets` check only two of them.

Odd parity, Hamming 8/4 and Hamming 24/18 are as the standard defines them, and an implementation MUST follow its encoding and decoding equations. NeoTeletext's choices are what happens to data that cannot be corrected.

WhereRule
Packet address, bytes 4 and 5A byte that cannot be corrected discards the whole packet and counts it.
Header, bytes 6 to 13The header MUST still end the page in reception. It MUST NOT start a page, and its display bytes MUST NOT feed the rolling header.
Editorial linkThat one link is invalid, and treated as no link.
Packet 8/30The field the byte belongs to is invalid.
Triplet in enhancement dataThe triplet is skipped, and does not move the active position.
Character failing parityBy default it does not replace a character that passed in an earlier reception of the same sub-page, unless the page was erased between them. A cell that never held a character that passed shows a space.

A single error in a Hamming 8/4 protection bit, P4 included, is reported as corrected, and the data is used. The sixteen Hamming 8/4 code bytes are those of clause 8.2. A Hamming 24/18 triplet is an uncorrectable one when the tests of clause 8.3 say so, or when the computed error position is above 24.

Bit-reversal between broadcast order and the order of a transport stream is applied only when reading a transport stream.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 8.1 Odd parity; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 8.2 Hamming 8/4; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 8.3 Hamming 24/18; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.2 Reserved bits

6.4 Packet address bytes

DOC-1752 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-349

A receiver MUST read a packet's magazine and packet number from its two Hamming 8/4 address bytes, and MUST discard the packet when either byte cannot be corrected.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.1.2 Packet address

6.5 The page header

DOC-1799 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-72, REQ-302, REQ-303, REQ-350

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-72 Selecting the character set of a page (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

A receiver MUST read a header's Hamming 8/4 bytes 6 to 13 as the page units and tens, the sub-code elements S1 to S4, and the control bits C4 to C14, keeping a separate validity for the page number, the sub-code and the control bits, and MUST keep the 32 display bytes 14 to 45 with their parity. The three bits of byte 9 and 11 that do not carry the sub-code carry C4, C5 and C6, and MUST NOT be read as part of it.

The national option is 4 × C12 + 2 × C13 + C14, so the three bits of the decoded byte are reversed to form it. Implementations often get this wrong, and two off-air recordings confirm it: the reversed order gives the wrong language.

Bytes 6 to 13 of a header are never displayed. Columns 8 to 39 of row 0 show header bytes 14 to 45.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1 Page header; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.1 Page number; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.2 Page sub-code; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.3 Control bits; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 2 Control bits in the page header

6.6 Display rows, X/1 to X/25

DOC-1800 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-242, REQ-351

A receiver MUST read bytes 6 to 45 of packets X/1 to X/25 as 40 seven-bit characters, each with its parity status. On an enhancement page the same packets hold triplets, not characters: see the packets of object pages.

A row not received shows 40 spaces.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.2 Packets X/1 to X/25; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause D.1.1 Missing packets X/1 to X/25

6.7 Enhancement packets, X/26

DOC-1801 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-352, REQ-358

A receiver MUST read byte 6 of packet X/26 as a Hamming 8/4 designation code, and bytes 7 to 45 as thirteen Hamming 24/18 triplets, each split into its 6-bit address, 5-bit mode and 7-bit data, and marked valid or invalid. Every valid triplet is one row or column function of Tables 28 and 29, with reserved modes reported as reserved. One decoding serves Level 1.5, Levels 2.5 and 3.5 and objects.

All sixteen designation codes are processed, 15 included, in the default profile. The SAA5246A profile ignores X/26/15, as that chip does.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.1 Packet X/26; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.1 Use of Packets X/26; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 27 Summary of Packet X/26 Triplet Functions; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 28 Function of Row Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets

6.8 Link packets, X/27

DOC-1802 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-120, REQ-286, REQ-316, REQ-353, REQ-359

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-286 Links: the four coloured keys and Index (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-316 Sub-codes in links (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

X/27/0. A receiver MUST extract the six links, computing each link's magazine by exclusive-OR of the packet's own magazine with its relative magazine bits, and MUST treat page number FF with sub-code 3F7F as no link. It MUST read the Link Control Byte, including the flag that displays row 24, and the two checksum bytes. The checksum MAY be used to check a page, and an encoder writing X/27/0 MUST compute it as clause 9.6.1 defines, with absent packets taken as spaces.

X/27/1 to X/27/3 are stored without interpretation. X/27/4 and X/27/5 are decoded as the compositional links of Levels 2.5 and 3.5, with a link holding an uncorrectable triplet marked invalid. X/27/6 to X/27/15 are ignored.

Links 1 to 4 of X/27/0 are the red, green, yellow and cyan keys, and link 6 the Index key. Link 5 is never followed by a viewer.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6 Packets for Page Linking; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.1 Packets X/27/0 to X/27/3 for Editorial Linking; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.2 Packets X/27/4 and X/27/5 - Format 1 - for compositional linking in presentation enhancement applications; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 14 Coding of Packet X/27/0-3; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 15 Coding of Packets X/27/4 and X/27/5, Format 1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause H.2 Packets with Y = 27 for automatic page acquisition

6.9 Page enhancement packets, X/28

DOC-1803 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-325, REQ-354, REQ-360, REQ-362, REQ-363

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-325 The page function decides how a page's numbers are read (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Packet X/28/0 MUST be read as Format 1 on every displayed page. Format 2 is never used for display. A receiver takes from it the page function and coding, the default G0 and G2 designation, the second G0 designation, the sixteen colour words of CLUTs 2 and 3, the default screen and row colours, Black Background Colour Substitution and Colour Table Re-mapping. In every multi-bit colour field the lowest-numbered bit is the least significant.

Each group of fields carries its own validity. A packet with an uncorrectable triplet in one group is absent for that group alone, and the next source of that group applies (see page-level presentation data).

PacketWhat NeoTeletext does with it
X/28/1Ignored. It carries DRCS colour tables, and DRCS is not decoded.
X/28/3Only the page function and coding are read, so that a DRCS page known only by this packet is never shown as text.
X/28/4The fields of X/28/0, with CLUT 0 and CLUT 1 as its sixteen colour words. The word for CLUT 1 entry 0 is ignored. Table 10 is followed where clause 9.4.7 says the reverse.
OthersIgnored.

The side-panel fields of these packets are read so that packets round-trip, and have no effect.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.2 Packet X/28/0 Format 1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.2.1 Page Function and Page Coding; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.2.2 Coding for basic Level 1 Teletext pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.4 Packet X/28/1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.6 Packet X/28/3; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.7 Packet X/28/4; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 3 Page function and page coding bits (packets X/28/0 Format 1, X/28/3 and X/28/4); EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 4 Coding of packet X/28/0 Format 1 for basic Level 1 pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 9 Coding of Packet X/28/3 for DRCS Downloading Pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 10 Coding of packet X/28/4 for basic Level 1 pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 32 Function of Default G0 and G2 Character Set Designation and National Option; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 33 Function of Second Language Designation and National Option Selection bits in packets

6.10 Magazine packets, M/29

DOC-1804 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-9, REQ-18, REQ-355, REQ-364

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-9 Magazine data (to be rechecked on livedevelopment); REQ-18 Magazine data at Levels 2.5 and 3.5 (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Packet M/29/0 with packet function 0000000 carries the magazine's copy of the fields of X/28/0, without the page function and coding. M/29/4 with the same function carries those of X/28/4. A packet with any other packet function, and M/29/1, is ignored.

A receiver MUST keep the latest valid M/29/0 and M/29/4 of each magazine, each reception replacing the packet of the same designation code, and MUST discard all of them when the service changes: when a format 1 packet 8/30 gives a network identification different from that of the last format 1 packet 8/30, whatever format 2 packets come between them, or when a new input starts. There is no time-out. M/29 packets MUST NOT start, end or attach to a page.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.5.1 Packet M/29/0; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.5.3 Packet M/29/4; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 11 Coding of Packet M/29/0; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 13 Coding of Packet M/29/4; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause F.3 Packets M/29

6.11 Packets 8/30 and 31, and broadcast service data

DOC-1805 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-356, REQ-455, REQ-456, REQ-457, REQ-458, REQ-459

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-458 Use of the initial page (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Packet 8/30 Format 1 gives the initial page with an absolute magazine, the network identification, the time offset, the Modified Julian Date, Coordinated Universal Time and a 20-character status message. Format 2 gives the initial page and status message, with its programme delivery data stored raw. A receiver reporting the time reports it as Coordinated Universal Time and as local time, and treats a digit out of range as making the date or time invalid.

The status message is given as text in the default G0 set without national option substitution. The initial page is the page shown when the decoder starts, and the target of the Index key when a page has no index link. With no packet 8/30 received, it is page 100.

A channel's initial page comes from channel.ttx. A live connection MAY also carry packet 8/30.

The teletext decoder counts and ignores packets numbered 30 in other magazines and all packets numbered 31. A port gives packets to the receiver of data channel 12 first. No packet 29 or 30 starts, ends or attaches to a page.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.7 General Coding of packets 30 and 31; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.8.1 Packet 8/30 Format 1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.8.2 Packet 8/30 Format 2; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 18 Coding of Packet 8/30 Format 1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 19 Coding of Packet 8/30 Format 2; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause D.3 Service level; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.9 Coding of packets 8/30, Formats 1 and 2; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.3 Elements of a magazine; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.4 Elements of the service

6.12 Numbering

DOC-1806 · section

6.12.1 Every address is accepted

DOC-1807 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-299, REQ-300, REQ-301, REQ-304, REQ-305, REQ-1031

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-300 Every address the standard defines is accepted (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-305 The null page address (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Verified by inspection. REQ-299 World System Teletext numbering only: Read at engine 6112177: `crates/neoteletext-core/src/limits.rs` is the one definition of magazines, page numbers and sub-codes, in EN 300 706's layout alone, and `packet/header.rs` decodes only that form. No test can show that a thing is absent: the requirement forbids the addressing of other systems, and it is checked by reading that none is there.

NeoTeletext numbers magazines, pages and sub-pages only as World System Teletext does. The addressing of the other systems of ITU-R BT.653 is not supported.

Every part of a NeoTeletext system MUST accept every page address the standard defines: magazines 1 to 8, page numbers 00 to FF, and sub-codes 0000 to 3F7F, each used as this section says. No part may refuse, drop or rewrite a page or sub-page because of its number, or because of how many sub-pages its page has. What a part keeps in memory or sends at once is a budget, which bounds memory and traffic and never which addresses exist.

A sub-page is identified by its magazine, page number and all thirteen bits of its sub-code. Sub-codes that differ only in S4 or S3 are different sub-pages, and MUST be stored, listed and served as such, unlike some receivers of other makers.

The address XFF:3F7F is the null page address. An encoder MUST refuse to write a header with it, and a decoder MUST treat one as ending a page and starting none. In a link it means no page is specified.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.1.2 Packet address; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.2.2 Page header packet; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.1 Page number; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.2 Page sub-code; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.1 Page numbering; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.10 Reserved page number; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.4 Elements of the service; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.1 Packets X/27/0 to X/27/3 for Editorial Linking; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.3 Packets X/27/4 to X/27/7 - Format 2 - for compositional linking in data broadcasting applications; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.8.1 Packet 8/30 Format 1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.8.2 Packet 8/30 Format 2; TR 100 287 V1.2.1 clause 10.1 Allocation of page numbers and page sub-codes

6.12.2 Reserved and recommended page numbers

DOC-1808 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-306, REQ-307, REQ-308, REQ-309, REQ-310, REQ-311, REQ-312, REQ-313

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-306 Page FF: time filling and terminating (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-310 Data pages use hexadecimal page numbers (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-311 Recommended page numbers (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-312 Page numbers ending in F (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-313 Pages a viewer can key (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

PageFunctionStatus
mFFTime filling and terminating headers. Never stored, listed, selected, served or published as a page.Reserved
mFEMagazine Organization TableReserved
mFDMagazine Inventory PageReserved
1BEAutomatic Channel InstallationReserved
1F0Basic TOP TableReserved
100Home page, unless packet 8/30 or channel.ttx names anotherRecommended
1DF, 1E7, 1F1 to 1F4EPG data, trigger messages, and the TOP data pagesRecommended

A channel, a kit or a server MUST NOT publish a display page on a reserved number, and a decoder reads a page on one only as its function. That leaves 8 × 253 − 2 = 2,022 page numbers in a service, shared by display pages and data pages alike. A receiver MUST NOT assume a recommended number: each function's page comes from the MIP, the Basic TOP Table or packet 8/30 when they give it.

A page number with a digit from A to F SHOULD be used for data pages, and the kits and the server give data pages such numbers by default. A channel SHOULD NOT put a display page on a number ending in F, and the kits warn when one does. A decoder MUST NOT decide what a page is from its number: its page function decides.

A viewer keys 100 to 899, 800 page numbers. Other pages are reached by links, and at Levels 2.5 and 3.5 stored for what they enhance.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.10 Reserved page number; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.15 Recommended and default page numbers; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.1 Page numbers for object definition and DRCS data pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.3 Elements of a magazine; TR 100 287 V1.2.1 clause 10.1 Allocation of page numbers and page sub-codes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.1 Page numbering; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.1 Overview of packet types; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 1 Summary of packet types, their function and application; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 annex L Use of Teletext Data for Automatic Channel Installation; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.2.2 Additional components; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.3 Magazine Inventory Page; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.6 Magazine Organization Table; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5 Public Enhancement Data; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.1 Object Definition Pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.2 DRCS Downloading Pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.2 Navigation via TOP; TR 100 287 V1.2.1 clause 10.5 Parallel magazine transmissions; TR 100 287 V1.2.1 clause 10.12 Packet X/28 format 1; EN 300 708 V1.2.1 clause 4.3.1.1 Page number

6.12.3 Sub-codes and how many sub-pages a page has

DOC-1809 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-290, REQ-314, REQ-315, REQ-316, REQ-317, REQ-318, REQ-319, REQ-328

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-290 Sub-pages (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-314 Sub-codes of display pages: the code of practice for automatic numbering, and 0000 beside others when a program gives it (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-315 The decimal sub-codes a viewer can key (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-316 Sub-codes in links (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-328 Every sub-page reachable; budgets bound memory, never what exists (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

The rules for a display page's sub-codes depend on who numbers them.

Automatic numbering. When a kit or a server chooses sub-codes, it MUST use the code of practice, through one function: one sub-page has 0000; 2 to 79 sub-pages have 0001 to 0079, each the decimal value of S2 and S1; a larger set has only sub-codes above 0079, the 3,120 decimal ones first and then the 4,949 with a hexadecimal digit, which is at most 8,069 sub-pages. A page that grows past 79 sub-pages, or falls back to 79, is renumbered.

A program's own sub-codes. A program or a TTI file that gives sub-codes has them kept exactly as given. They may be any of 0000 to 3F7E. Sub-code 0000 beside others is the page's own content, a peer of the others, and first in order.

The limit. A display page has at most 8,190 sub-pages, whoever numbers it: 8,192 values less 0000 and 3F7F, the bound Table 24 states for code 51. A page that uses 0000 has at most 8,189 others. Every part MUST accept, hold, list and serve such a page; every sub-page is reachable, and a store is a cache.

Keying. A viewer enters a sub-page by four decimal digits: 0001 to 3979, with S2 at most 7, 3,199 sub-codes.

Time codes. A page's sub-code may carry the time, as Annex E.2 describes. A page the MIP marks with code 79 keeps only its latest version, and its sub-codes are not listed as sub-pages. Without a MIP it is any page.

A page linked to MUST have sub-codes up to 3F7E, because in a link 3F7F names no particular sub-page, and a key whose link is the null address is disabled.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.2 Page sub-code; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.1 Page numbering; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.3.3 Page Function Coding; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 24 Magazine Inventory Page (MIP) Codes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.2 Page Time-Code; TR 100 287 V1.2.1 clause 10.1 Allocation of page numbers and page sub-codes; TR 100 287 V1.2.1 clause 10.6 FLOF and TOP Navigation Protocols'; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.1 Packets X/27/0 to X/27/3 for Editorial Linking; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.8.1 Packet 8/30 Format 1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.8.2 Packet 8/30 Format 2; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.3.4 Sub-page range

6.12.4 Sub-codes that are not sub-pages

DOC-1810 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-320, REQ-321, REQ-322, REQ-323, REQ-324, REQ-325, REQ-326, REQ-327

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-320 Sub-codes of enhancement data pages (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-324 The Magazine Inventory Page (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-325 The page function decides how a page's numbers are read (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-326 TOP's page numbers (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-327 Sequence, serial and parallel (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

The page function decides how a page's numbers are read: a basic page by the rules for display pages, an enhancement data page and a data broadcasting page as below. A page with no X/28/0 is a basic Level 1 page, unless the MOT, the MIP or the Basic TOP Table names it as something else.

Enhancement data pages (POP, GPOP, MOT and MIP, and DRCS pages although NeoTeletext does not read them) code the sub-code nnXs: nn is the last packet sent, X an update indicator, and s, S1 alone, names the sub-page. So such a page has at most 16 sub-pages, and a MOT or MIP has one. An encoder MUST write these sub-codes, and a store keys these pages by S1. They MUST NOT be listed as sub-pages.

Data broadcasting pages have no sub-pages: the sub-code is a data stream identifier, a continuity index and a last packet. A decoder MUST NOT list, step through or count them. Such a page is known by page function 0001 or the MIP's codes E0 to E4, and is not decoded.

TOP tables are stored and served as the data pages they are, at the numbers the Basic TOP Table gives, and never offered as display pages. NeoTeletext does not navigate by TOP.

The MIP. A decoder MAY read a MIP's page codes and thirteen-bit counts. Table 24 lists FA to FC as reserved and then FC as the trigger message page; NeoTeletext takes the second.

In serial mode a page ends at the next header of any magazine, and in parallel mode at the next of its own. C11 MUST be the same in every header of a service, and a server MUST set C9 on a page it sends out of numerical order.

Not built. A server does not write a MIP, and does not derive C9: a program sets it.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.2.1 Page Function and Page Coding; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 3 Page function and page coding bits (packets X/28/0 Format 1, X/28/3 and X/28/4); EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.1 Page numbering; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.6 Rules for the transmission of presentation enhancement data; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table B.1 Indicating Fragmented and Partial Pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.6.1 Page Format; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.2 Navigation via TOP; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.3.2 Format of the Magazine Inventory Page; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.3.4 Sub-page range; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 24 Magazine Inventory Page (MIP) Codes; EN 300 708 V1.2.1 clause 4.3.1.2 Subcode; EN 300 708 V1.2.1 clause 4.4.1 Page header; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.2.1 Definition of a page; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.1.1 Page Format; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.2.1 Page Format; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.2 Transmission order of packets with Y = 26, 27 and 28; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5 Public Enhancement Data; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.6.4 Object Links; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.2 Packets X/27/4 and X/27/5 - Format 1 - for compositional linking in presentation enhancement applications; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.1.4 Page Number Identification; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.2.2 Page Number Identification; TR 100 287 V1.2.1 clause 10.7 Preferred use of packets 27/4; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.15 Recommended and default page numbers; 6.12.5 One definition of the limits

DOC-1811 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-329

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-329 One definition (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

The numbers of this section are written once, in the engine's module neoteletext_core::limits. The server and the browser import them. A program in another language is given them by the server, and no part writes one as a literal.

6.13 Receiving pages

DOC-1812 · section

6.13.1 Where a page starts and ends

DOC-1813 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-1, REQ-2, REQ-3, REQ-135, REQ-140

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-140 Tolerance of the broadcaster's ordering rules (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

A decoder keeps, for each magazine, the page being received, and whether the service is serial or parallel, from C11 of the latest valid header. A header whose magazine, page number, sub-code and control bits are all valid starts that page. Packets 1 to 28 then attach to it: in parallel mode, those of the same magazine; in serial mode, those of the same magazine before the next header of any magazine. In serial mode, packets 1 to 28 of another magazine are discarded and counted.

Reception of a page ends, in serial mode, at any header of any magazine, and in parallel mode at a header of the same magazine. This follows clause 7.2.1 and Annexes B.3 and B.4, not the simplified wording of Table 2. A header with an invalid page number or control bytes, and a page FF header, end a page and start none.

A page is also complete when 100 milliseconds pass with no packet for it. That time is configurable. Rows are shown as they arrive.

Pages not stored. A magazine's reception is idle, receiving a page, or absorbing a page that is not to be stored, such as a hexadecimal page, whose rows are dropped silently and attach to nothing else. In serial mode every header reaches every magazine, because any header ends the page in progress, and in parallel mode a header reaches only its own. Packets 29, 30 and 31 never reach this reception. A row packet with no page in progress is discarded and counted.

Order. Nothing depends on the ordering rules the standard places on a broadcaster. Packets 26, 27 and 28 may arrive after or among the rows of their page, designation codes in any order or with gaps, and rows in any order. The gap after a header may be shorter than 20 milliseconds. Triplets may address positions out of screen order, magazines may be interleaved in any pattern parallel mode allows, and the Magazine Organization Table and object pages may arrive before or after the pages that use them. A page received so gives the same stored page and cell grid as the same packets in standard order, once all have arrived. Order matters only where the standard makes it matter: which header starts and ends a page, which of two overwrites of a cell comes later, and which of two contending attributes comes later in transmission (Annex C.2, rule 3).

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.2.1 Definition of a page; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.3 Elements of a magazine; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.3 Serial mode transmission; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.4 Parallel mode transmission; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.9 Maximum time between successive packets of a page; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 2 Control bits in the page header; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.7 Transmission sequence of enhancement data for page updates; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause C.2 Rules for determining priorities of spacing and non-spacing attributes

6.13.2 What is stored, and how a page is updated

DOC-1814 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-4, REQ-5, REQ-7, REQ-8, REQ-10, REQ-11, REQ-12, REQ-13

Each sub-page is stored by its full sub-code. For each, a store keeps the header's address, control bits and display bytes with parity, rows 1 to 25 with parity and which were received, up to sixteen packets X/26, X/27/0 as decoded links, X/27/1 to X/27/3 raw, X/27/4 and X/27/5, X/28/0 Format 1 and X/28/4, and of X/28/3 the page function and coding alone. X/28/1 is read past and not kept.

A header with C4 set clears the stored rows 1 to 28 of that page and sub-code, its enhancement and link packets included, before new rows are stored. Without C4, a sub-page received again merges: each received byte replaces the stored one by the parity policy of byte coding, and rows not received keep their content.

When a page with C8 is received, or a displayed page changes, a viewer program is told. A store's capacity is the caller's. When it is full, the least recently received page is replaced first.

Pages with a hexadecimal number are processed for page termination like any other. At Levels 1 and 1.5 they are not stored, unless a diagnostic option asks. At Levels 2.5 and 3.5 they are, and viewers still cannot key them.

The rolling header and clock show while a page is being searched for: columns 8 to 31 from incoming headers, of every magazine in serial mode and the requested magazine in parallel mode, excluding headers with C9 or rejected by the error rules; columns 32 to 39 from every valid header, always.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.3 Control bits; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.2 Use of control bits C4 (Erase Page) and C8 (Update Indicator); EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.3 Serial mode transmission; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.4 Parallel mode transmission; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.14 Real-time clock Page Header Packets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 1 Summary of packet types, their function and application

6.13.3 Pages that are not shown as text

DOC-1815 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-14, REQ-15, REQ-348

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-348 Enhancement pages are never shown as text (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

A page whose X/28/0 Format 1 or X/28/3 gives a page function other than 0000 (basic Level 1 page), or a page coding other than 000 (7 bits with odd parity), MUST be flagged as not displayable as text and not rendered as a text page. Object pages, DRCS pages and the Magazine Organization Table never receive Level 1 national option or display semantics. On them, C12 means a continued fragment and C13 a partial page.

Pages with C5 or C6 are stored like others, and shown in the boxed display of boxes. Each reception replaces the previous content, so subtitles update as sent.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.2.1 Page Function and Page Coding; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 3 Page function and page coding bits (packets X/28/0 Format 1, X/28/3 and X/28/4); EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.6 Rules for the transmission of presentation enhancement data; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table B.1 Indicating Fragmented and Partial Pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.3 Control bits

6.13.4 Enhancement pages

DOC-1816 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-16, REQ-17, REQ-19, REQ-20, REQ-21, REQ-22

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-17 Hexadecimal pages at Levels 2.5 and 3.5 (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-21 Updates of enhancement pages (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

At Levels 2.5 and 3.5 a store keeps object pages, the Magazine Organization Table, and every other page whose function is not a basic Level 1 page, DRCS pages among them. Their rows are stored as received: each reception replaces the stored bytes of the packets it carries, with no merge by parity, because their bytes are not parity-coded. This applies to a page whose X/28/0 or X/28/3 function says so, to page xFE, and to a page a stored MOT or X/27/4 and X/27/5 link names as an object page. A page that becomes one after it was first stored is stored as received from its next reception.

Enhancement pages have a part of the store of their own, sized by the caller. Its documented default is the totals of Annex E.8: 500 packets at Level 2.5 and 1,200 at Level 3.5. When it is full, the least recently received enhancement sub-page goes first. Nothing is discarded when a MOT changes.

Updates follow Annex B.6 and Table B.1: C4 on a full transmission erases first; a partial transmission and a continued fragment replace only the packets they carry; a change of the update indicator S2 is reported, so decoded data is not reused; and the last-packet number is kept.

The Level 1 page is shown at once and never waits for enhancement pages. When an enhancement page, a MOT or an M/29 that the displayed page uses arrives or changes, the page's grid is built again and its changed rows are redrawn.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.6 Rules for the transmission of presentation enhancement data; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table B.1 Indicating Fragmented and Partial Pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.1 Page numbers for object definition and DRCS data pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.8 Limits for global enhancement data; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.7 Transmission sequence of enhancement data for page updates

6.13.5 The packets of object pages and the Magazine Organization Table

DOC-1817 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-365, REQ-366

Object pages. For a stored packet X/1 to X/25 of an object page, byte 6 is Hamming 8/4 coded and the rest is thirteen triplets. Bit 1 of byte 6 of packets 1 to 4 says whether the packet carries pointer data. A pointer packet holds 24 nine-bit pointers in its triplets 1 to 12, counted from 0: bits 1 to 9 to the even-numbered object, and bits 10 to 18 to the odd-numbered one. Triplet 0 is reserved. An uncorrectable pointer triplet makes both of its pointers unused.

An absolute triplet number t from 0 to 506 is triplet t mod 13 of packet 3 + t / 13 when t is below 299, and otherwise triplet (t − 299) mod 13 of X/26 with designation code (t − 299) / 13.

The Magazine Organization Table. Every byte of page xFE is Hamming 8/4 coded. From X/1 to X/14 a decoder takes the byte pair of any page, at the position Figure 20 gives. X/19 and X/20 hold the GPOP and POP links at Level 2.5, and X/22 and X/23 at Level 3.5, with their fallback bits and default objects, and X/21 and X/24 the totals of object pages. The DRCS links, the DRCS total and the second byte of each pair are not read. An uncorrectable byte makes its link or pair invalid.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.1.1 Page Format; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.5.1.2 Pointer Table; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.6.1 Page Format; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.6.2 Object Page Association; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.6.4 Object Links; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.6.6 Number of Enhancement Pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 20 Object link start positions within MOT; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 21 Coding of object links within MOT

6.13.6 Decided, not built: request mode, pre-fetching and the strict options

DOC-2123 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-6, REQ-289, REQ-370

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-6 Page store in request mode (not implemented on livedevelopment); REQ-289 Pre-fetching linked pages (not implemented on livedevelopment); REQ-370 Alternative and strict options (not implemented on livedevelopment).

Not built. No code does any of this, and nothing can rely on it.

Request mode. A page store may also work like the SAA5246A's. A configured number of page requests, four by default, is active at any time, and only pages matching a request are stored. Each request names a magazine and a page number, and optionally a sub-code pattern in which each of the four digits may be marked "don't care".

Pre-fetching. In request mode, when a page with Fastext links is displayed, a decoder should place requests for all six linked pages, link 5 included, in the spare acquisition slots, so that the coloured keys respond at once, as Annex H intends. On a web service, pre-fetching fetches only from the service of the page being viewed.

Strict options. Independent of the profile, these alternatives are to be available: the row-setting rule of clause 12.3.2, a single Start Box, an exact framing code, and ignoring out-of-order triplets.

Rests on: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 annex H Code of Practice for navigation via FLOF; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.2 Active Position

7 Teletext presentation

DOC-1784 · section

7.1 Presentation levels

DOC-1818 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-246, REQ-256, REQ-367, REQ-368, REQ-369, REQ-371, REQ-372

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-368 The "SAA5050" profile (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-369 The "SAA5246A" profile (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

NeoTeletext implements Presentation Levels 1, 1.5, 2.5 and 3.5 as one ordered setting. The level is a setting of the receiver, and changing it needs no page acquired again.

LevelWhat runs
1The page is decoded from its Level 1 bytes alone. Packets X/26 are stored and not applied.
1.5The Level 1 page, then the Level 1.5 pass.
2.5The Level 1 page, then the enhancement processor in place of the Level 1.5 pass.
3.5As Level 2.5, with packets X/27/5, X/28/4 and M/29/4, and Modified G0 and G2 designation with no limit. These are ignored at Level 2.5.

The default profile, "en300706", presents at Level 3.5. The "saa5050" profile presents at Level 1, with 24 rows, one fixed national character set, codes 0/0 and 1/0 as spaces, and momentary reveal. The "saa5246a" profile presents at Level 1.5, with X/26/15 ignored, row 24 shown whenever X/24 is present, and 12 by 10 cells. Every setting stays changeable after a profile is applied.

What a build holds. A receiver may leave capabilities out, and a page then shows as it would on a receiver without them. The engine's default build holds Levels 1 and 1.5, the non-spacing attributes and the Colour Map of both levels. Objects and Additional Flash Functions are features a build names. neottx takes the default, so it draws no objects unless built with them. The browser's decoder builds objects, and not Additional Flash Functions.

Not implemented at any level: DRCS, the font styles and side panels.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.1 Basic Teletext - Presentation Levels 1 and 1.5; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10.2 Enhanced Teletext - Presentation Levels 2.5 and 3.5; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12 Presentation Levels; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 25 Summary of Display Components and their application; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 1 Summary of packet types, their function and application; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.3 Backwards compatibility in X/26 addressing; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 annex H Code of Practice for navigation via FLOF

7.2 The cell grid

DOC-1819 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-226, REQ-261, REQ-373, REQ-388, REQ-1643

Verified by inspection. REQ-373 The cell grid as the primary output: Read at engine 6112177: `cell_line`, `render_row`, `render_line` and `render_screen` in `crates/neoteletext-core/src/render/` take a grid and no packet, and the other renderers are built on them. Its tests build grids by hand, which shows a renderer needs nothing but the grid, and no test can show that no renderer decodes packets itself.

Decoding a page gives a grid of 25 rows by 40 columns, at every level, and every other output is drawn from it. A cell records its character as a character set and a seven-bit code, never as Unicode; its foreground and background colours; flash; conceal; box membership; its size role; and whether it shows a held mosaic, came from an overwrite, or failed parity.

At Levels 2.5 and 3.5 a cell also records its colours as Colour Map indices 0 to 31, a vertical and a horizontal size part, underline, invert, its flash mode, rate and phase, the window membership, and which source wrote its character. The grid also holds the page's Colour Map, screen colour and row colours.

A renderer MUST NOT decode packets. Every feature that crosses rows is resolved in the grid, so a row draws from its own cells alone, in any order, and the same grid draws the same pixels on every platform.

7.3 Level 1

DOC-1820 · section

7.3.1 Rows and spacing attributes

DOC-1821 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-138, REQ-227, REQ-228, REQ-229, REQ-230, REQ-237, REQ-238, REQ-244

Each row is processed from column 0 to column 39 from the start-of-row state Table 26 gives: alphanumeric white, black background, steady, normal size, box off, not concealed, contiguous mosaics, hold off, the held mosaic a space, and the default G0 set. Nothing carries from one row to the next.

Every attribute of Table 26 that has a Level 1 function MUST be implemented with its timing:

TimingCodes
Set-at: takes effect in its own cell0/9, 0/C, 1/8, 1/9, 1/A, 1/C, 1/D, 1/E
Set-after: takes effect from the next cell0/1 to 0/7, 0/8, 0/A, 0/B, 0/D, 1/1 to 1/7, 1/B, 1/F

A cell holding an attribute shows as a space in the state in force for that cell, except where hold mosaics substitutes the held mosaic. Colour number n has red when bit 0 is set, green when bit 1 is set and blue when bit 2 is set. ESC toggles the G0 set from the next cell.

Black Background and New Background take effect from their own cell, which they paint.

Codes with no Level 1 function. The default treats 0/0 and 1/0 as alpha black and mosaic black, set-after attributes with a black foreground, because services send them. Codes 0/E and 0/F show as spaces and change no state at Levels 1 and 1.5. The "saa5050" profile shows 0/0 and 1/0 as spaces.

The order in a cell. Each cell is handled in five steps, in this order: apply a set-at attribute; choose what the cell shows, an alphanumeric character from the current G0 set with national option substitution, a mosaic from the G1 set or the G0 character for blast-through codes, or for an attribute a space or the held mosaic; record the cell with the current colours, flash, conceal, box and size; update the held mosaic, which a mosaic character with bit 6 set becomes, kept with its contiguous or separated form; and apply a set-after attribute. Normal Size resets the held mosaic if the size changes, a change of mode resets it, any colour ends conceal, and Double Height resets it if the size changes and marks the row as containing double height. Start Box takes effect only when the next cell also holds Start Box. The row after a double-height row is not processed from its own bytes at all.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.2 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 15.5 Spacing attributes

7.3.2 Mosaics and hold

DOC-1822 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-74, REQ-231, REQ-232

In mosaic mode, codes 2/0 to 3/F and 6/0 to 7/F are block mosaics, recorded with the contiguous or separated form in force at the cell, and codes 4/0 to 5/F are blast-through G0 characters with national option substitution.

Hold mosaics follows Table 26 exactly. While hold is active in mosaic mode, each attribute cell shows the held mosaic. The held mosaic is the latest mosaic of the row with bit 6 set, kept in the form it had when stored. Blast-through characters are never held. It resets to a space at the start of the row, on any change between alphanumeric and mosaic mode, and on any change of size. Repeating the current size, and turning hold on or off, do not reset it.

The standard leaves the moment of a reset open. When a set-after attribute changes mode or size, its own cell shows the state before the change, so it can still show the held mosaic, and the reset follows that cell. A set-at Normal Size resets before its own cell is shown.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 47 G1 Block Mosaics Set; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 15.7.1 G1 Block Mosaics Set; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause G.3.3 Hold Mosaics, Release Mosaics and Separated Mosaic Graphics

7.3.3 Double height

DOC-1823 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-233

Characters after a Double Height attribute have their top half in the row and their bottom half in the row below, with the attributes in force at the top half. When any cell of a row is double height, the transmitted content of the next row is ignored: that row shows bottom halves below double-height characters, and background colour with no foreground below normal-height ones.

Double height in rows 0, 23 and 24 MUST be treated as normal height. Annex C.3 leaves this to the decoder, and normal height is NeoTeletext's choice.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause C.3 Rules applying to the size attributes

7.3.4 Flash

DOC-1824 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-234, REQ-412

Cells after a Flash attribute, until Steady or the end of the row, are flashing. The standard gives the Level 1 code no rate. NeoTeletext takes the SAA5050's, at every presentation level: shown for 48 fields and hidden for 16, which is 960 milliseconds and 320 milliseconds. In the hidden phase foreground pixels are drawn in the background colour. The timing is configurable.

The rates of Table 29 apply only to Additional Flash Functions. The level in force never changes Level 1 Flash.

Flash timing comes from the caller's clock, and is worked out once a frame.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets

7.3.5 Conceal and reveal

DOC-1825 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-235, REQ-292

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-292 Reveal, mix and zoom (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Cells after a Conceal attribute, until the next alphanumeric or mosaic colour attribute or the end of the row, are concealed. They show as spaces keeping their background colour, unless the viewer's reveal setting is on. Reveal is latched by default, and clears when another page is selected. Momentary reveal holds for 160 milliseconds after the last request.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes

7.3.6 Boxes, and newsflash and subtitle pages

DOC-1826 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-236, REQ-245, REQ-378

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-245 Viewer display modes (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

A box starts only when two Start Box attributes are in consecutive cells, and at the second. A setting allows a single Start Box to start one from the next cell. End Box ends a box from the next cell.

Box membership affects display only on a page with C5 or C6, or in a viewer display mode that uses it. On such a page the receiver switches to boxed display by itself: only boxed areas are drawn, and the rest is the picture. Where there is no video beneath, the picture is transparent pixels, or a configurable fill colour in a format with no transparency. Viewer modes are normal, mix (foreground everywhere and background transparent everywhere) and boxed.

Which of the foreground or background, row or screen colour, and picture each pixel shows at Levels 2.5 and 3.5 is Table C.1 (see boxes and windows).

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause G.1 Mixed mode operation of decoders; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause G.3.1 Start Box and End Box; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.13 Use of Start and End box spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table C.1 Display Priorities

7.3.7 Row 0 and the status area

DOC-1827 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-239, REQ-281, REQ-418

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-239 Row 0 (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-418 The receiver-owned status area cannot be forged (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Row 0 is built by the receiver. Columns 0 to 6 are its status area, by default the page number requested or shown, such as P100. Column 7 is an alphanumeric colour attribute it generates: white when the page is found, green while searching. Columns 8 to 39 come from header bytes 14 to 45, of the displayed page or, while searching, of the rolling header. The row is then decoded like any other, from the default state at column 0.

Row 0, columns 0 to 7, belong to the receiver alone. Nothing a page transmits may write there: not the header's bytes, not a Level 1.5 overwrite, not an object, a row colour or a screen colour. A write addressed there MUST be discarded, and the rest of the data it came from processed. Every security indicator, such as the mark of an unencrypted connection, is shown only there, and stays shown on a page with C7 (Suppress Header).

A page with C7 shows no row 0, apart from the status area when configured and any active indicator.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.3 Control bits; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.7 Character cells addressable by objects; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 2 Control bits in the page header

7.3.8 Row 24, and hidden rows

DOC-1828 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-240, REQ-241, REQ-287

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-240 Row 24 (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-287 The receiver draws the link row (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

By default row 24 is displayed only when the page carries X/27/0 with the flag that displays row 24. Otherwise it stays blank, available for the receiver's messages, even when packet X/24 arrives: a server sends a page's row 24 whatever the flag, so a receiver decides by the flag. On a page whose links come from the TTI Link Extension the receiver draws row 24 itself, and ignores any the page sends (see navigation). The "saa5246a" profile shows row 24 whenever the page carries packet X/24.

A page with C10 (Inhibit Display) shows no rows 1 to 24. Row 0 is unaffected.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause H.2.4 Link Control Byte; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause H.3 Packets with Y = 24; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.3 Control bits

7.3.9 Navigation

DOC-1829 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-284, REQ-285, REQ-286, REQ-287, REQ-288, REQ-290, REQ-291, REQ-293, REQ-295

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-286 Links: the four coloured keys and Index (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-287 The receiver draws the link row (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-288 Following a link to another service in `neottx view`: the viewer asks before the first use of an origin (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-290 Sub-pages (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-295 Back and forward history (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Page numbers. A viewer enters three digits, the first 1 to 8 and the others 0 to 9, shown as they are entered in the status area. Digits that cannot form a page number are rejected. Next and previous page go in numerical order or to the next page present, a configured choice, and 899 wraps to 100.

Links. A page has at most four links, one for each coloured key, plus Index. On pages from packets they come from X/27/0. On pages from TTI files and channels they come from the lines of the TTI Link Extension, which the page encoder also turns into X/27/0. A null or absent link leaves its key without effect. A target with sub-code 3F7F requests the page, and any other sub-code that sub-page. On a page with no links, the Index key requests the initial page.

The link row. For links of the TTI Link Extension, the receiver draws row 24 from the link data: four prompts of 10 columns in the order red, green, yellow, cyan, each a colour attribute in the key's colour and a label of at most 9 characters, on black, with nothing from the page. A link to another service begins with », from the G2 set, leaving 8 characters of label.

Other services. In neottx view, and any other port that takes its keys through the engine's viewer, a coloured key leading to a service not yet confirmed this session is not followed at once. The status area shows that the receiver is asking, row 24 shows the destination's name and origin, the same key goes, and any other key cancels. The confirmation belongs to an origin. In the NeoTeletext browser there is no such question: a link or coloured key that leads to another channel takes the tab there at once, with Back to return. Not built: neottx view's row 24 shows the service's short name, not its display name or origin, and it holds the confirmation by the service's place in the descriptor's list, not by origin.

Sub-pages. A viewer can step through the sub-pages of a page and enter one directly. The status area shows the place and the count, and row 0's seven columns show P100/12, 100/123 past 99, and 100/+++ past 999.

Hold, home and history. Hold keeps a page from updating until released. Home requests the initial page. History holds the service, page and sub-page of each page shown, 32 entries by default, and selecting a new page clears the forward history.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.1 Packets X/27/0 to X/27/3 for Editorial Linking; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 11.1 Navigation via FLOF; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause H.4 Fall-back conditions; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.1 Page numbering

7.4 The Level 1.5 pass

DOC-1830 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-247, REQ-248, REQ-249, REQ-250, REQ-251, REQ-252, REQ-253, REQ-254, REQ-255

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-251 Character-writing modes (to be rechecked on livedevelopment); REQ-253 Overwrite rules (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

At Level 1.5 the stored packets X/26 of a page are processed in ascending designation code, skipping codes not received, and within a packet triplets 1 to 13 in order, to the first valid Termination Marker. The active position starts at row 0, column 0.

The row. By default the receiver follows Annex A.3: every row triplet but the Termination Marker sets the row, address 40 meaning row 24 and 41 to 63 rows 1 to 23, and mode 00111 with address 63 sets row 0, column 0. A strict setting follows clause 12.3.2: only modes 00001, 00100 and 00111 set the row. Annex A.3 is the default at Level 1.5 alone.

The column. Every column triplet sets the active column, except modes 00100, 00101 and 01010 and mode 00110, which leave the position unchanged.

Characters. These modes write at the active position, from the default sets of the page, with no national option and no effect from ESC. A value below 2/0 writes nothing and still sets the column.

ModeWrites
00010A G3 character
01111A G2 character
10000A G0 character, with 2/A writing @
10001 to 11111A G0 character with the diacritical mark numbered by the mode's low four bits

Every other mode has no display effect. That includes 01011, the Level 2.5 form of G3, and the Programme Delivery Control modes. A triplet addressing a position earlier than the one before it is still processed. A strict setting ignores it.

Overwrites. The standard is silent on how an overwrite meets the Level 1 page, and NeoTeletext's rules are these:

  1. Level 1 attributes are processed first from the raw bytes, as if no X/26 existed.
  2. An overwrite replaces only the glyph. The character keeps the cell's colours, flash, conceal, box membership and size.
  3. It draws as an alphanumeric-style glyph even in mosaic mode.
  4. It never changes the held mosaic, and wins over a held mosaic in its cell.
  5. On the top half of a double-height character it draws double height.
  6. Addressed to the lower row of a double-height row, it is not displayed.
  7. It shows whether or not the Level 1 byte there passed parity.
  8. Of two overwrites of a cell, the later wins.
  9. In row 0 it applies when the page is displayed, not to headers rolling during a search.
  10. Addressed to row 0, columns 0 to 7, it is discarded.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.1 Use of Packets X/26; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.2 Active Position; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.4 Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.3 Backwards compatibility in X/26 addressing; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.4 Addressing order for enhancement triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause D.1.3 Packets X/26 with non-sequential designation codes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 28 Function of Row Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.3 Use of packets X/26 in Level 1.5 transmissions; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause E.10 Characters including diacritical marks

7.5 Levels 2.5 and 3.5

DOC-1831 · section

7.5.1 The enhancement processor

DOC-1832 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-257, REQ-258, REQ-259, REQ-260, REQ-282, REQ-283, REQ-1358

The enhancement processor runs after the Level 1 grid is built, at Levels 2.5 and 3.5 only. It processes the page's stored X/26 packets in ascending designation code, skipping missing ones, within each its triplets 1 to 13, up to the first Termination Marker or Object Definition; then each object at the point something invokes it; and, when the page has no X/26 that carries a display triplet, the default objects.

Principles for what the standard leaves open. Normative text beats informative text, and tables beat prose. Where the standard is silent, documented hardware of the time decides, and then the most literal reading. No software decoder is a reference for any behaviour.

The active position starts at row 0, column 0. Annex A.3 does not apply at these levels, because invocations carry object numbers in their address field. Only these row triplets move the row: Full Row Colour, which also sets column 0; Set Active Position, which sets the column from its data when that is below 40, and leaves it otherwise; and Address Display Row 0 with address 63, which sets row 0, column 0. Addresses are as at Level 1.5.

Every column triplet sets the column, except reserved modes 00100, 00101 and 01010 and Programme Delivery Control mode 00110. A character mode with data below 2/0 sets the column and writes nothing.

Damage and order. An uncorrectable triplet is skipped without moving the position. A triplet addressing a position earlier than the one before it from the same source is applied where it addresses. An Origin Modifier not followed at once by an invocation is discarded. Any triplet with address 63 and mode 11111 ends the data it is in.

Reserved values. Reserved modes, the Programme Delivery Control modes and reserved data values have no display effect, and move the position only as above. Column mode 00101 is also NeoTeletext's control triplet, which draws nothing.

Faults are contained. A missing, damaged or unusable enhancement source removes only what depends on it, and is counted. The Level 1 page is always shown.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.1 Use of Packets X/26; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.2 Active Position; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.4 Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.2 Reserved bits; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.3 Backwards compatibility in X/26 addressing; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.4 Addressing order for enhancement triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 28 Function of Row Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets

7.5.2 Characters at Levels 2.5 and 3.5

DOC-1833 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-262, REQ-263

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-262 Characters written at Levels 2.5 and 3.5 (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Column modeWrites
00001A G1 character
00010, 01011A G3 character
01001A G0 character of the current G0 set
01111A G2 character of the current G2 set
10000 to 11111A G0 character with the mode's mark, 10000 with 2/A writing @

National option substitution and ESC never apply to these. A G3 character does not change the held mosaic, and hold never replaces a character written by enhancement data.

A G1 character in columns 2, 3, 6 or 7 is a block mosaic, contiguous or separated by the non-spacing Underline or Separated attribute at its cell, never by Level 1 codes 1/9 and 1/A. In columns 4 and 5 it is the current G0 set's character at that position, with no national option.

The undefined positions, G3's 6/E, 6/F, 7/E and 7/F and the undefined Latin G2 positions, show a space and trace a diagnostic.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.4 Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 47 G1 Block Mosaics Set; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 25 Summary of Display Components and their application; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 15.4 Modified G0 and G2 Character Set Designation; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes

7.5.3 Non-spacing attributes

DOC-1834 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-265, REQ-266, REQ-267, REQ-268

Foreground colour, background colour, flash, and each of the six display attributes (double height, double width, boxing or window, conceal, invert, and underline or separated mosaics) are separate attribute types. Where two of one type apply to a cell, the latest wins under Annex C.2: by the cell where each takes effect, then where each is placed, then by order of transmission. A non-spacing attribute at the same cell as a set-at Level 1 attribute of its type wins, though read literally rule 3 would give it to the Level 1 attribute, since X/26 is sent first.

The Foreground Colour and Background Colour triplets set their colour to the Colour Map index in D4 to D0, from their cell to the end of the row or a later attribute of the type. Data with D6 or D5 set only sets the column. Neither changes alphanumeric or mosaic mode.

The Display Attributes triplet (01100) sets all six from its data bits at its cell, and a 0 bit also turns off what a Level 1 attribute turned on. D3 is ignored. In particular:

  • Conceal lasts until a Display Attributes triplet clears it or the row ends. A Level 1 colour code does not end it.
  • Underline or separated mosaics underlines G0, G2 and G1 columns 4 and 5, and draws G1 mosaics separated. Level 1 code 1/9 does not cancel it. Clearing it also clears a Separated Mosaics from 1/A.
  • Invert swaps the displayed colours. While it is in force, a Level 1 change of foreground alters the displayed background, and the reverse.

At these levels 0/0 and 1/0 set entry 0 of the foreground CLUT. 0/1 to 0/7 and 1/1 to 1/7 set entry n of the CLUT Colour Table Re-mapping selects. 1/C sets entry 0 of the background CLUT, or the row's full row colour under Black Background Colour Substitution. 1/D sets a background from the background CLUT, with the entry of the foreground colour the Level 1 page selected. A black background from 0/0 or 1/0 and then 1/D is not substituted, and neither is a colour set by a triplet.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3 Non-spacing attributes and additional characters; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 27 Summary of Packet X/26 Triplet Functions; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause C.2 Rules for determining priorities of spacing and non-spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.7.1 Overlapping rules

7.5.4 Size

DOC-1835 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-269, REQ-270

Double height, double width and double size are available as spacing attributes 0/D, 0/E and 0/F and as non-spacing attributes, and any size attribute ends any other. Sizes resolve by Annex C.3:

  1. Double height and double size do not take effect in rows 0, 23 and 24, and double width and double size not in column 39. The character is drawn without the doubling that cannot be shown.
  2. Earlier size attributes in scan order take priority.
  3. Where an enlarged character conflicts with an earlier one, the conflicting doubling is dropped. Double size falls to normal only if neither double width nor double height alone fits.
  4. All attributes at the origin of an enlarged character apply to all its parts.
  5. Attributes hidden by an enlarged character take effect at the first position after it, unless superseded.

The row below. When a row contains spacing double height or double size, the row below shows lower halves, and elsewhere the local background of the cell above with no foreground, with its own Level 1 content ignored. Enhancement characters written to that row, in cells not covered by lower halves, are displayed. For local data and Active and Adaptive objects, their attributes are the start-of-row defaults, changed only by earlier enhancement data on that row.

When a row's only double height comes from non-spacing attributes, the row below is not suppressed.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 26 Spacing attributes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause C.3 Rules applying to the size attributes

7.5.5 Additional Flash Functions

DOC-1836 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-271, REQ-412

Column mode 00111 sets, at its cell, the flash mode from D1 and D0 and the rate and phase from D4 to D2, as Table 29 defines them. Data with D6 or D5 set, and rates 110 and 111, only set the column. It is a feature a build names, and without it the triplet only sets the column.

The standard gives two rates and names the modes, with no duty cycle and no meaning for the phases. NeoTeletext divides one second into twelve units, numbered 0 to 11:

  • Slow flash is on in units 0 to 8 and off in 9 to 11.
  • Fast flash, in each half-second of six units with phase p of 1, 2 or 3, is off in units 2(p − 1) and 2p − 1 and on otherwise.
  • Incremental flash gives successive characters from the triplet's column phases 1, 2, 3, 1 and so on, and decremental flash 1, 3, 2, 1 and so on.
  • Normal flash shows the background in the off phase, invert phase swaps the phases, and adjacent-CLUT flash shows the colour whose index is the foreground's exclusive-OR 8.

The attribute lasts to the end of the row, or the next flash attribute. It acts on the foreground only, and never changes Level 1 Flash.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets

7.5.6 Boxes and windows

DOC-1837 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-273

The boxing or window bit of a Display Attributes triplet starts a boxed area on a page with C5 or C6, and a window on any other page. Inside a window the full row colour becomes transparent. A Level 1 Start Box on a page with neither bit does not open a window: boxing keeps its Level 1 meaning.

The source of each pixel follows Table C.1.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause C.1 Relationship between the display planes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table C.1 Display Priorities

7.5.7 The Colour Map

DOC-1838 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-274, REQ-739

Each page shown at Level 2.5 or 3.5 has a Colour Map of 32 entries of twelve-bit colour, starting from Table 30. Every colour in the grid is an index 0 to 31, equal to 8 × CLUT + entry, so the Level 1 colours keep their numbers. CLUTs are numbered 0 to 3, as in Table 30; Figure 28's numbering 1 to 4 is not used.

CLUTsReplaced byLevel
2 and 3The page's X/28/0 Format 1, or when absent or invalid the magazine's M/29/02.5, 3.5
0 and 1The page's X/28/4, or when absent or invalid the magazine's M/29/43.5

Index 8, CLUT 1 entry 0, is always transparent, and its twelve bits are ignored. Table 10 is followed where clause 9.4.7 says the reverse, and the heading of a row of Table 8 is followed where its text contradicts it. A default black means Colour Map entry 0.

Colour Table Re-mapping applies to the Level 1 spacing colours and to the start-of-row white and black, unless Black Background Colour Substitution replaces the background.

The NeoTeletext browser shows a page in this Colour Map as the page's packets define it, and Colour Table Re-mapping, Black Background Colour Substitution and the default row colour of Table 4 with it.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.4 Colour Map; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 30 Colour Map; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 4 Coding of packet X/28/0 Format 1 for basic Level 1 pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 8 Coding of Packet X/28/1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 10 Coding of packet X/28/4 for basic Level 1 pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.7 Packet X/28/4; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 25 Summary of Display Components and their application; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 28 Function of Row Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 29 Function of Column Address triplets

7.5.8 Page-level presentation data

DOC-1839 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-275

The default screen and row colours, Black Background Colour Substitution and Colour Table Re-mapping each come from the first valid source in this order:

  1. the page's X/28/0 Format 1;
  2. the page's X/28/4, at Level 3.5;
  3. the magazine's M/29/0;
  4. the magazine's M/29/4, at Level 3.5;
  5. for substitution only, the fallback bit of the page's POP link in the MOT, clear with no POP link;
  6. the defaults of Annex D.2.1.

The standard states the page's precedence over the magazine for each pair of packets, not across pairs, and not for damage. NeoTeletext's rule is that the page beats the magazine, and a group of fields decoded with an uncorrectable triplet counts as absent for that group alone.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4.2 Packet X/28/0 Format 1; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.5.1 Packet M/29/0; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause D.2.1 No packet M/29/0; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause C.4 Interpretation of character set and DRCS table changes at enhanced levels; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.5.3 Packet M/29/4

7.5.9 Screen colour, row colours and borders

DOC-1840 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-276, REQ-277, REQ-278, REQ-740

The screen colour is, highest first: a Full Screen Colour triplet; the page's default screen colour; the magazine's; Colour Map entry 0.

A row's colour is, highest first: Full Row Colour triplets in transmission order; the screen colour, only when a Full Screen Colour triplet set it; the page's default row colour; the magazine's; Colour Map entry 0. A default screen colour from X/28 or M/29 does not replace the default row colour. This reads "the screen colour already invoked" of Annex A.5 as the triplet, the only reading under which Table 4's default row colour can take effect.

Full Screen Colour, row mode 00000 with D6 and D5 at 0, sets the index in D4 to D0, does not move the position, and is ignored with other data. It should be the first triplet that defines anything on the page, once a page, at address 41.

Full Row Colour, mode 00001, sets the position. With S1 S0 at 00 it colours its own row. At 11 it colours that row and every row below, to row 23, or 24 when row 24 is displayed. Address Display Row 0 with address 63 does the same for row 0. At 01 or 10 either sets the position and no colour. Commands apply in transmission order, so an earlier fill continues below a later "this row only".

Both are acted on only in local enhancement data and Active objects. The screen colour fills the areas above row 0 and below the last displayed row, as Table 28 says, and left and right of the rows, which Table 28 does not mention and NeoTeletext rules (DEC-569). No row colour is drawn in row 0, columns 0 to 7.

In the NeoTeletext browser. Each page is drawn inside a border of its screen colour, one character cell wide at the sides, and links and cells are found in the page's image, not its border. While a tab shows one page alone, that page's screen colour is the background of the whole window below the trusted strip, behind the magazines, the page lists and the overview. The trusted strip never takes it, because a channel must not be able to paint what the browser alone says. Text and marks drawn on the colour take black or white, whichever has the greater contrast. With the wall of pages showing, or before the page has arrived, the background is the browser's own.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.5 Rules for row and screen colours; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 4 Coding of packet X/28/0 Format 1 for basic Level 1 pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 28 Function of Row Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3.3 Row Address triplets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 30 Colour Map; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table C.1 Display Priorities

7.5.10 Objects

DOC-1841 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-330, REQ-331, REQ-332, REQ-333, REQ-334, REQ-335, REQ-336, REQ-337, REQ-338, REQ-339, REQ-340, REQ-341, REQ-342, REQ-343

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-335 The body of an object (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-338 Adaptive objects (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-340 Display priority (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Objects are built by a build that names the objects feature. Without it, Object Invocation triplets place nothing.

Invoking. An Object Invocation (row modes 10001 Active, 10010 Adaptive, 10011 Passive) names its source by S1 and S0: 01 local, 10 the page's POP, 11 its GPOP. Source 00 is skipped. The object's origin is the active position plus the offsets of an Origin Modifier that immediately precedes it: row offset the address less 40, and column offset the data, 0 to 71. Every coordinate inside is relative to the origin, Address Display Row 0 and row address 40 included. When the object ends, the active position returns to its value at the invocation.

Finding it. For a local object, N7 to N4 give the X/26 designation code and N3 to N0 the triplet. An invocation is skipped when N8 is 1, the triplet number is 13 to 15, or the packet was not received. For a public or global object, the sub-page is the one whose S1 equals N3 to N0; the pointer packet is 1 + (N8 N7); the pointer triplet, from 0, is 1 + 3 × (N6 N5) + k, with k 0, 1 or 2 for Active, Adaptive and Passive; N4 chooses bits 1 to 9 or 10 to 18; and the pointer is an absolute triplet number. The object is absent when the pointer is 0x1FF, is 507 to 510, points into a pointer packet or packets 1 or 2, or its pointer packet is missing.
