A page converts to packets in the order of Annex B.2, and a set of pages in the order of Annex B.7, except that a DRCS page goes after object pages.

The page function. A function carried by an OL,28 line wins over a PF line, and the lowest designation code wins among them, whatever the order of the lines, so that a page read, written and read again is unchanged. A function other than 0 gives X/28/0 in the form of Table 6, whose marker 11111111100 of bits 8 to 18 is read bit 8 first. A basic page with RE, or with a coding other than 0, gives the form of Table 4 with every other field at its default, the colours of Table 30 among them. PF,0,0 alone gives no packet, so that it does not override a magazine's M/29/0. Page codings 1, 4 and 5 are sent as parity text.

Page mFF carries the magazine packets: it is sent as a time-filling header after its M/29 packets, and converting a stream to TTI puts each magazine's stored M/29 packets back on it. Converting an enhancement page with no X/28 writes rows that all pass parity as parity text, and otherwise takes the page to be a POP with coding 2.

Defined by: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.2 Transmission order of packets with Y = 26, 27 and 28; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause B.7 Transmission sequence of enhancement data for page updates; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 3 Page function and page coding bits (packets X/28/0 Format 1, X/28/3 and X/28/4); EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 4 Coding of packet X/28/0 Format 1 for basic Level 1 pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 6 Coding of Packet X/28/0 Format 1 for other types of pages; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 table 30 Colour Map; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.1 Page numbering; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 13.8 Default Objects; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.4 Page enhancement data packets; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 10 System Components for Presentation

9.11 The TTI Link Extension

DOC-1879 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-91, REQ-177, REQ-183, REQ-286, REQ-287, REQ-1494

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-91 The page checker (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-177 TTI page files (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-286 Links: the four coloured keys and Index (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-287 The receiver draws the link row (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

A TTI page declares links with these lines, which NeoTeletext defines. Each applies to the page or sub-page of the latest PN line.

LineMeaning
LN,key,target,labelA link of key R, G, Y, C or I, each at most once. The label is at most 9 characters, 8 for a link to another service, and is ignored for I.
LK,mpp,targetLinks a page number the page shows. Typing that number on this page follows the link. At most eight a page, and a ninth is ignored with a diagnostic.
LT,row,column,length,targetLinks a run of characters in rows 1 to 23, within the 40 columns. At most eight a page, and a ninth is ignored with a diagnostic.

A target is one of:

  • mpp or mpp:ssss: a page, and optionally a sub-page, in this channel, in hexadecimal.
  • name:mpp or name:mpp:ssss: a page in a service channel.ttx names. An unknown name makes the link null.
  • An address: a channel's folder, or a page file in one. It uses http or https, is printable ASCII with no spaces or commas, and is at most 8,000 bytes.
  • web: followed by an http or https address: a page on the web, for a receiver to open outside teletext once the user agrees. It is allowed in LN and LT, never in LK.

A target that breaks these rules makes the link invalid. A fifth coloured LN line, or a repeated key, makes the page's links invalid, and the page shows with none.

The page encoder writes the links to the same service into X/27/0, with others written as null, and a row 24 with the "display row 24" flag. Links to another service, addresses, web: targets, and LK and LT lines cannot be carried in a page's packets, and reach a live receiver as a LINKS record.

Every link to a page in the same service SHOULD have its page number visible in the page's rows, and neottx check warns when one does not. A warning is marked considered with a line CK,considered,name, which other TTI readers ignore.

9.12 The structure decided and not built

DOC-1985 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1889, REQ-1891, REQ-1892, REQ-1896, REQ-1899, REQ-1900, REQ-1901, REQ-1902

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1889 Validators and versions. (tested, not recorded as met on main); REQ-1891 Titles. (not implemented on main); REQ-1892 Magazine lists. (not implemented on main); REQ-1896 Derived files. (tested, not recorded as met on main); REQ-1899 A single resource. (not implemented on main); REQ-1900 This computer. (not implemented on main); REQ-1901 Presentation. (not implemented on main); REQ-1902 What presentation never changes. (not implemented on main).

Not built. The owner decided a structure of a channel on 30 October 2026. What is left of it is below, recorded in requirements, and nothing can rely on it. The items from hand-written and built channels onwards give it in full.

DecidedWhat exists today
A reader that shows a page's list from its parts, and holds to one versionneottx asks for part 0 and a sub-page's file, and reads no row. The browser takes these names and parts with its next engine.
A validator sent as an ETagA validator and a page's version in each part of a list, and nothing sent as an ETag
A sub-page's title taken from row 1 when none is givenA title is given or absent
M.pag, a magazine's list of pagesNothing
NNN.ep1 among a channel's page filesEP1 is read only as a file opened by itself
A command that builds a channel, and its magazine listsThe engine's writer lays out a page's files and derives its list, and no command calls it
A file of any name opened as a single resource, at most MAX_SINGLE_RESOURCENo such bound
A presentation member of channel.ttx, "teletext" or "site", which no member can use to hide the receiver's own indicatorsThe member is refused as unknown

9.13 Other files a receiver may read

DOC-1872 · reference · normative · requirements REQ-169, REQ-170, REQ-171, REQ-172, REQ-173, REQ-179, REQ-180

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-171 Finding teletext in a transport stream (to be rechecked on livedevelopment).

These are not files of a channel. A receiver that opens a file by itself decides its format by its content, and an explicit format overrides that. Page files begin with commands such as PN, or DE,, transport streams have 0x47 every 188, 192 or 204 bytes, elementary streams begin 00 00 01 BD, and EP1 files FE 01. For a headerless stream, a decoder tries each packet length and bit order and takes the one in which addresses decode best.

EP1 is a 6-byte header, an optional enhancement block when the flag is 0xCA, 25 rows of 40 bytes, and a 2-byte terminator. A file without enhancements is 1,008 bytes.

Transport streams. A reader accepts 188, 192 and 204-byte packets, regains synchronisation after corruption, and discards packets with the transport-error flag. It accepts packets with both an adaptation field and payload, though clause 4.1 permits only one. It finds teletext from the Program Map Table by stream type 0x06 with a teletext descriptor, and reports each entry. In an elementary stream packet with stream identifier 0xBD it accepts data identifiers 0x10 to 0x1F and 0x99 to 0x9B, reads data units 0x02, 0x03 and 0xC0, checks the framing code, and bit-reverses each of the 42 bytes. Other data units are skipped by their length.

edit.tf and zxnet addresses. A page may arrive in an address, as a metadata digit, a colon, and base64url of 25 rows of seven-bit codes.

Defined by: EN 300 472 V1.3.1 clause 4.1 Transport Stream (TS) packet format; EN 300 472 V1.3.1 clause 4.2 PES packet format; EN 300 472 V1.3.1 clause 4.3 Syntax for PES data field; EN 300 472 V1.3.1 clause 4.4 Semantics for PES data field; EN 300 472 V1.3.1 table 2 Syntax for Data_field for EBU Teletext; EN 300 472 V1.3.1 table 3 data_identifier; EN 300 472 V1.3.1 table 4 data_unit_id

9.14 Hand-written and built channels, and what is not built of them

DOC-2034 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1886, REQ-1893, REQ-1896, REQ-1903, REQ-1904

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1886 Finding a page. (tested, not recorded as met on main); REQ-1896 Derived files. (tested, not recorded as met on main); REQ-1903 Media types. (tested, not recorded as met on main); REQ-1904 Bounded readers. (tested, not recorded as met on main).

Not built. M.pag, NNN.ep1 in a channel, and the build tool. This item gives the structure of a channel decided on 30 October 2026 in full, and the rest of it is built: page folders and sub-page files and a page's list say how. The structure decided and not built says what is left.

Principles. One structure is used unchanged by files on disk, an SD card, a file:// folder, any static host and the server. Every file a reader asks for is named from a page number, a sub-code or a part number. Every file has a maximum a microcontroller can hold, and no file grows with the size of the channel but the magazine lists, which the standard's page count bounds. What can be derived is generated, never written by hand: a list says what its files hold, and the file is the truth.

The files of the structure, where P is a part number in decimal, 0 to 127, with no leading zeros, and M a magazine, 1 to 8:

NameHoldsLargestTier
channel.ttxThe channel's settings16,384 bytesOptional
contents.txtThe table of contents65,536 bytes, 1,000 linesOptional
index.t42The first sub-page of any number of pages, so a channel opens with one request262,144 bytesBuilt
M.pagMagazine M's pages17,204 bytesBuilt
NNN.ttiPage NNN, with all its sub-pages262,144 bytesHand-written
NNN.t42Page NNN: all its sub-pages, or only the first when the page has a folder262,144 bytesEither
NNN.ep1Page NNN, one sub-page: a 6-byte header, sixteen 40-byte enhancement packets, 25 rows of 40 bytes and a 2-byte terminator1,648 bytesHand-written
NNN/SSSS.t42Sub-page SSSS of page NNN3,108 bytesBuilt
NNN/SSSS.ttiSub-page SSSS of page NNN, as TTI262,144 bytesHand-written
NNN/P.subPart P of page NNN's list of sub-pages4,119 bytesBuilt
pushThe live connectionOne message 65,536 bytesOptional

A reader asks for these names and no others, and every name fits the 8.3 form a minimal FAT library reads. Every reader of one of these files refuses whole a file larger than its largest, and allocates no more than that bound. M.pag and NNN/P.sub are accepted as text/plain or application/octet-stream, NNN.ep1 as application/octet-stream, NNN/SSSS.t42 as NNN.t42 is, and NNN/SSSS.tti as NNN.tti is.

Hand-written. A folder with page files, NNN.tti, NNN.t42 or NNN.ep1, each holding the whole page, and optionally channel.ttx and contents.txt. Nothing else is needed, and a reader MUST open such a folder fully: it learns a page's sub-pages from the page's file as it decodes it. EP1 and edit.tf files carry no trusted page number, and in a channel the file's name gives it.

Built. What a generator writes, from a hand-written folder or from a program's pages: every page as NNN.t42, and a page of more than one sub-page with its folder NNN/ holding every sub-page's file and the parts of its list; the magazine lists; and index.t42. A built channel MAY also carry the hand-written files. Nobody writes a validator, a part or a magazine list by hand: a generator derives them from the page data, every time it writes them. A generator puts first sub-pages in index.t42 in ascending page order, and stops before the page that would pass the bound.

The build tool. neottx turns any folder into a built channel: a vbit2 service folder, captures, editor exports, files of any name with the page number read from each TTI's PN line or each EP1's name.

Finding a page. A reader looks in its store, when the page is there and confirmed current; then in NNN.tti, NNN.t42 and NNN.ep1, stopping at the first that answers 200 and passes its format's check; otherwise the page is not found, at a cost of at most three requests. Within a channel the extension that last answered 200 is tried first. A file that answers 200 and fails its check is a failure of that page, and the others are not tried.

9.15 Validators, versions and sub-page titles, and what is not built of them

DOC-2035 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1889, REQ-1891, REQ-1896

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1889 Validators and versions. (tested, not recorded as met on main); REQ-1891 Titles. (not implemented on main); REQ-1896 Derived files. (tested, not recorded as met on main).

Not built. A built file's ETag, and a title taken from row 1 or carried in M.pag. A file's validator, a page's version and a list derived from its pages are built, and every part of a page's list carries them.

  • A file's validator is the first 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of the file's bytes, as the engine's Validator::of writes it.
  • A page's version is the validator of the page's sub-pages' packets end to end, in the page's order: what NNN.t42 would hold if it held them all. It changes when any sub-page changes, is added or is removed.
  • A built file's ETag MUST be its validator in double quotes, so that a sub-page's validator in its list and the ETag of its file are one value. A reader that holds a sub-page compares the list's validator with the ETag it holds, and fetches only what differs, with no conditional request for the rest. A generator that cannot set headers, on a static host, gives up only that saving: the host's own ETag still serves conditional requests.
  • A generator derives every part, magazine list, validator, version and title from the page data each time it writes them. A reader treats a sub-page's file as the truth when it disagrees with a list.

A sub-page's title is, in this order:

  1. the title its publisher gave it: a program's sub-page title, or a TTI page's DE line for its first sub-page;
  2. otherwise the text of its row 1: each character shown in an alphanumeric mode whose code is 0x20 to 0x7E is kept as that ASCII character, and everything else, spacing attributes and mosaic characters included, becomes a space. Runs of spaces become one, the ends are trimmed, and the result is cut to 40 characters;
  3. otherwise, when that leaves nothing, it has none, and a list shows its sub-code and place alone.

Row 0 is never used: its text is the service's header, the same on every sub-page. A page's title, in M.pag, is its first sub-page's.

9.16 Decided, not built: magazine lists

DOC-2039 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1892

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1892 Magazine lists. (not implemented on main).

Not built. Nothing reads or writes M.pag.

M.pag lists magazine M's pages, so that a reader knows which pages exist, how many sub-pages each has, whether it changed and what it is called, without asking for any page. It says what the standard's Magazine Inventory Page says, with the exact counts the inventory gives only as ranges above 79, and titles.

The format is text, as a list of sub-pages is, with no header: the number of lines is the number of pages. Every line is one page, in ascending page number:

FieldHolds
pageNNN, a page of magazine M: any page number but the reserved ones, MFD, MFE and MFF in every magazine, and 1BE and 1F0
sub-pagesIts number of sub-pages in decimal, 1 to 8,190
versionThe page's version
titleOptional: 1 to 40 characters, its first sub-page's title

Size. A magazine has at most 253 pages, a line is at most 3 + 1 + 4 + 1 + 16 + 1 + 40 + 2 = 68 bytes, and a magazine list is at most 253 × 68 = 17,204 bytes. All eight are at most 137,632 bytes, and a reader needs only the magazine being browsed.

Faults. A line of another magazine's page, a reserved page, a page not above the line before it, or an invalid field is ignored and counted. A magazine list that answers 404 or 410 means the channel does not list that magazine, not that it is empty: a reader then finds its pages by asking for them.

With contents.txt. contents.txt is the channel's own table of contents, in the order and sections it chooses, of at most 800 keyable pages. The magazine lists are the inventory of every page, hexadecimal ones included. A reader shows the first and navigates by the second.

9.17 Decided, not built: opening any address

DOC-2036 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1898, REQ-1899, REQ-1900

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1898 Opening any address. (tested, not recorded as met on main); REQ-1899 A single resource. (not implemented on main); REQ-1900 This computer. (not implemented on main).

Not built. What a receiver does today is in opening a channel and other files a receiver may read. The order below, the bound of a single resource and a folder on this computer as a channel by this structure are decided and not built.

A reader opens any address it is given. It decides what the address is in this order, and stops at the first that fits.

#The addressOpens as
1Its fragment is a page in the edit.tf or zxnet editors' form, a hexadecimal digit, a colon and the base64url page. Never on an http or https address, where a fragment is refusedThat page alone, decoded from the address, with nothing fetched
2A folder: its path ends in /A channel
3A channel's folder followed by one of the fixed names of the structureThat channel, at the page or sub-page the name gives: a page's or sub-page's file name is normalised to its place in the URL map. A receiver's file that names no page, such as index.t42, is refused as a viewer's address, and a program told to open one opens its channel
4Any other address that answers 200 with teletextA single resource, in every reader but the browser
5Any other address that answers 200 with a web pageIn every reader but the browser, discovery: its Link headers and <link> elements lead to a channel
6A live feed in the zxnet viewer's WebSocket formThat live stream
7Anything elseRefused, saying why

In the browser every http and https address must be an address of the URL map when it is read, so what would be case 4 or 5 falls to case 7, refused with a sentence for the viewer, with no tab opened and no fetcher started. The address field alone adds a channel's missing final slash first. The security rules apply to every case, whatever it opens as: the plain-HTTP mark, no downgrade, the local-network checks, strict HTTP, and the rules of redirects.

A single resource is a file that is not part of a channel, with any name, anywhere.

  • Its format is decided by its content, never by its name or extension: a T42 recording, a transport stream, sliced VBI data, a TTI file or an EP1 file. Of these the browser reads T42 and TTI alone, and neottx and the engine's readers keep the others. It holds whatever pages, sub-pages and enhancements it holds.
  • Its identity is its own address, not a folder. It has no settings, no lists, no live connection and no cookies. Its links lead only to pages it holds, and any other link is a navigation away from it, shown as one.
  • It is read whole, at most MAX_SINGLE_RESOURCE, 64 MiB, in the process that reads network data, and played to the decoder as a local file is.
  • It is never interactive. Taking the viewer's keys needs a channel.

Folders and files on this computer. A folder on this computer is a channel by the same structure, with the same names and rules, and a folder that holds no file of the structure opens as an empty channel at its initial page, which is not found. A file on this computer is a single resource, whatever its name. In the browser a file is its own channel with no cookies, read as T42 or TTI alone, any other format being refused, and it is loaded at once. A file: address takes the places of the URL map after #, the one use of #.

9.18 Decided, not built: presentation

DOC-2037 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1901, REQ-1902

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1901 Presentation. (not implemented on main); REQ-1902 What presentation never changes. (not implemented on main).

Not built. channel.ttx has no member presentation today, and one is refused as unknown.

A member presentation of channel.ttx, "teletext" or "site", by default "teletext", says how a reader shows the channel. It changes nothing about the structure.

  • "teletext": the reader's whole interface: the overview of the channel's pages, the contents, the list and count of a page's sub-pages, and keying page numbers.
  • "site": the channel is shown as a site of linked pages. By default the reader hides the overview, the contents, and the list and count of sub-pages, and keying digits does not select a page: they go to the channel when it takes keys, and do nothing otherwise. The viewer moves only by what the pages offer: their links, their Fastext keys, the prompts they make clickable, and back and forward through the pages they have seen. Every page keeps its address and can be bookmarked and shared.

It is one switch, not a member a panel, so that no channel can make a half of one and half of the other.

What presentation never changes. No member, file or page of a channel hides, moves or changes the reader's trusted indicators and controls: its strip and the address it shows, row 0 columns 0 to 7, which belong to the receiver alone, what says keys go to the channel, and the keys the reader keeps for itself. The viewer stays in charge: the reader's own menu still opens the overview and the sub-page list of a "site" channel.

Presentation is not secrecy. Every file is still at its fixed address, and index.t42 and the lists name every page to anyone who asks. A page only some viewers may see is sent by a program to those viewers, over the live connection.

9.19 Decided, not built: fetching a page's enhancement pages

DOC-2128 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-511

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-511 Enhancement dependencies (not implemented on livedevelopment).

Not built. No reader does this. When a viewer shows a page of a channel at Level 2.5 or 3.5 and its enhancement pages are not in the store, it is to ask once for its magazine's Organization Table, page xFE, and then for the object pages the table and the page name. Pages already stored are not asked for again, and these requests do not change which page is kept current. While they load the page shows with what has arrived, and is drawn again as they arrive. A dependency that cannot be fetched removes only what depends on it.

10 The program protocol

DOC-1787 · section

10.1 A program behind a channel

DOC-1964 · concept · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1474, REQ-1476

A channel's program runs outside the server, in any language, and one program serves every viewer of its channel, multiplexed by connection and session. It never sees packets, unless it asks to send them raw, or connections' sockets, headers or cookies' raw form. The server relays: a program owns every page, and the server holds only what each open connection is, and the last sub-page it sent it, and for a minute what a page shows the web.

The conversation is one JSON object a line, in both directions. A viewer's selection reaches the program as a request that stays open: the server sends selected, the program answers with a sub-page, and may send that sub-page again at any time as it changes, until the viewer selects something else. A channel can differ for every viewer, because the program answers each connection as it chooses.

The kits for TypeScript and Python are conveniences over this protocol: it is the interface, and a program in any language can speak it directly.

10.2 Processes, transports and lines

DOC-1965 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1034, REQ-1042, REQ-1043, REQ-1045, REQ-1475, REQ-1479

Verified by inspection. REQ-1045 One protocol on every transport: Read at neoteletext-app-server 3572de8: `crates/teletext-native/src/apps.rs` has one `relay`, generic over its reader and writer, called for standard input and output and for the Unix socket, and `Channel::from_program` is told nothing of the transport. No test can check this: a test runs one relay and shows nothing about whether a second could be written.

The protocol's version is 6. Two transports carry the same lines.

  • Standard input and output. The server starts each channel's program as the channels file says, in its own folder, with NEOTELETEXT_CHANNEL set to the channel's folder. A program's standard error becomes the server's log, a line an event, cut at 64 KiB.
  • A Unix domain socket. A channel named with a socket is served to the program that connects to it, which runs as a service of its own and so outlives a restart of the server. One program at a time. When it goes, or the server restarts, it connects again and is sent start again.

Lines. One JSON object a line (RFC 8259), UTF-8, ending in a line feed, at most 16 MiB. A program MUST flush each line as it writes it. Every message is an object tagged by type. Every line a program sends is checked whole and refused whole when it is not valid: the program is sent refused, and nothing of the line is acted on. An unknown type or field is refused, never skipped, and so is a field given twice.

The exact contract is a JSON Schema, draft 2020-12, generated from the server's types and published as protocol/schema-6.json of neoteletext-app-server: its $defs/ServerMessage is what the server sends, and its $defs/ProgramMessage what a program sends.

Starting and ending. When a program starts or connects, the server sends it start, then connected for every connection open. While no program runs, a channel has nothing to show, and each selection ends with DENIED 503. A program that ends is started again after 1 second, then 2, 4 and so on up to 30. One that ran 5 seconds or more was ended, not failing, and is started again at once.

Queues. The network loop never waits for a program. At most 4,096 lines wait for it, and beyond that the newest are dropped and the server's log says so. Lines for a program that is not running are dropped, since it is told of every viewer when it starts. Timers are a program's own: it has a clock.

The channels file is a JSON object with one member, channels, a list of objects. No member sets a channel's initial page, which is 100 on the NeoTeletext server, and a member not named here is ignored. Each has a folder of letters, digits, - and _, a name, and either a command, a list, and a directory, taken from the file's own folder when relative, or a socket:

{"channels":[{"folder":"example","name":"Example","command":["python3","program.py"],"directory":"example"}]}

Not built. A program on a WebSocket, for a program elsewhere, is a requirement of its own and does not exist.

10.3 Common shapes

DOC-1966 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1475, REQ-1476, REQ-1502

ShapeMeaning
connectionA whole number from 1, below 253, naming one open connection: one tab, with one selection. Never reused while the server runs.
sessionA whole number below 253: the viewer's session, which outlives connections and is shared by a viewer's tabs
pageThree hexadecimal digits in capitals, the magazine 1 to 8 first, "100" to "8FF"
subFour, "0000" to "3F7F", with no bit outside the thirteen, where "3F7F" means the page and none of its sub-pages in particular
requestA whole number from 1: the number of a connection's selection
connections"all", every open connection, or a list of 1 to 4,096 connections. A connection named that has closed is passed over.
Text, in every fieldUTF-8 in Unicode normalisation form C, with no control character and no bidirectional formatting character, within the field's length
BytesExact base64 (RFC 4648 section 4), padded, with no white space

A connection's selection is a page with sub"3F7F", answered with the page's first sub-page in ascending sub-code order, which then becomes the selection; or one sub-page. A sub-page never changes by itself.

10.4 The request number

DOC-1967 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1473, REQ-1481

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1473 The program may send to any connections it names at any time; the server forwards to those connections, a sub-page or a sub-page list only where its page is selected, and keeps nothing (to be rechecked on livedevelopment); REQ-1481 Every selection has an ending: when a program does not answer in time, or is not running, the viewer is told the channel is unavailable and the connection stays open (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

The server gives each selection on a connection a rising number, from 1, and passes it to the program in connected and selected. The program's reply, page, subpages, redirect and denied for that selection carry it back as request. The server drops any whose number is not the connection's current selection, and counts it as stale_answers. So a late answer cannot replace the page now on screen, and a program that was slow did nothing wrong: it is not refused.

This is between the server and a program. The viewer's input protocol does not carry it.

  • reply, redirect and denied MUST carry a number. page and subpages may carry null, which says it is a change.
  • A reply to a selection already replaced still gives the index, since that is the connection's, and its sub-page is dropped.
  • A number belongs to one connection, so a page or subpages that carries one names exactly one, and one that names others is refused with the rule one_connection.
  • A page with a request of null is a change: it reaches connections whose selection it shows, and that have had their answer. A connection still awaiting its answer is not sent it, since that answer will be current.
  • A redirect for a selection already replaced is dropped: the viewer's own choice overrides it. A redirect to a page is itself a new selection, with a new number.

The ending.denied ends a selection at once. A reply whose selected is null does not end one: it says only that the page goes separately, and a page, redirect or denied must follow, or the deadline does. A redirect to another channel ends one.

10.5 From the server to a program

DOC-1968 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1033, REQ-1053, REQ-1471, REQ-1472, REQ-1476

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1033 Applications are told the limits (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

typeFieldsWhen
startprotocol (6), epoch_ms, standard, budgetsThe program started or connected. connected follows for each connection open.
connectedconnection, session, request, cookies, a list of name and value pairs, at most 50, each within 4,096 bytes, without the server's own; selection, page and sub, or null for the channel's initial page; path, that selection as an address names it, or null; query, the query of the address selected, as written, or nullA connection opened and its first selection came, or 1.5 seconds passed with none. Answer with reply.
selectedconnection, request, path, 302 or 302/0002, page, sub, queryThe connection selected a page, sub 3F7F, so send its first sub-page and its subpages list; or one sub-page, so send that
inputconnection, page, sub, lost, eventInput on the page on screen. page and sub are what the viewer was looking at, the connection's selection, except that a release names the page that held it. lost is true on a release the page lost.
leftconnection, pageThe connection's selection left a page, or the connection closed
closedsessionThe session ended, 5 minutes after its last connection closed. Forget it.
statsserver, channelEvery 10 seconds: the server's and the channel's figures
refusedmessage, its type or null; path of the first fault, such as page.rows[3]; rule; detail, in wordsA line the program sent was refused whole, and nothing of it was done
describepage, and nothing else: no connection, session, cookie or queryA web visitor or a search engine is at the page's address, or the program has just started: the server asks once then, for page 100 and with no visitor, and any answer makes the channel ready. Answer with description, built from no viewer: nothing says who asked, and the answer goes to everyone for up to a minute. See describing a page.

standard is the standard's maxima, so that a program holds none of its own: magazines 8, pages 2,022, pages_per_magazine 253, pages_in_magazine_1 251, keyable_pages 800, subpages 8,190, decimal_subpages 3,199.

budgets is the server's own: line_bytes, 16 MiB; title_characters, 120; author_log_bytes, 200; log_characters, 1,000; named_connections, 4,096; reply_limit_ms, how long the server waits for an answer to connected or selected; description_text_characters, 16,000; description_limit_ms, how long a web visitor waits for a description.

rule is one of syntax, line_too_long, unknown_field, missing_field, duplicate_field, wrong_type, not_listed, out_of_range, too_long, too_short, not_ascending, duplicate, reserved, wrong_magazine, forbidden_character, not_nfc, not_base64, wrong_length, not_in_index, unknown_connection, not_awaited, wrong_page, outside_profile, invalid_packet and one_connection.

stats holds in server: uptime_s, connections, connections_peak, connections_total, messages_sent, bytes_sent, requests, restarts, memory_kb, channels, and viewers by channel folder. In channel it holds viewers, viewers_peak, viewers_total, opens, most_opened, inputs, keys, pages_sent, refused, redirects, sound_records, lines_to_application, lines_from_application, lines_dropped, sessions_waiting and sessions_resumed. Each of the two also carries all_time. Nothing in it identifies a viewer.

Rests on: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.1 Packets X/27/0 to X/27/3 for Editorial Linking

10.6 What a program hears of input

DOC-1969 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-207, REQ-1476, REQ-1557

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-207 Held input is released on focus loss, pointer leaving, selection change and connection loss, each release marked as lost input, never deselecting the page (to be rechecked on livedevelopment).

event.kind is one of these. Every key, click, move and hover goes, and a program ignores what it does not use.

kindFields
keyaction, press, repeat or release; key, the name in the last column of the code table, or one character; text and base, or null
mouseaction, press, release or move; button, 0 left, 1 middle, 2 right, null on a move; buttons, left, middle and right on a move, else null; column, 0 to 39; row, 0 to 24
wheeldx, dy in 1/120ths of a line, down positive; column, row
hoveraction, enter or leave; the control's first column and row
text, pastetext
focus, blurNone

Input stamped with anything but the connection's selection is dropped before it reaches a program, except a release. A lost mouse release is no click. A program selects pages for a viewer with redirect.

A button above 2 is not passed on, and a wheel's delta is held to 16 bits.

What a server does not yet pass a program says what is missing from this list.

10.7 What a server does not yet pass a program

DOC-2126 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1347, REQ-1360

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1347 The server passes a channel program every field the receiver sends: key modifiers, the mouse's dot position and modifiers, and input-method composition (COMPOSE) (not implemented on main); REQ-1360 The server passes each session's pointer moves to its channel program through one slot, the latest replacing any not yet taken, at most 60 a second, never acknowledged (not implemented on main).

Not built. Two things a receiver sends do not yet reach a program as they should.

Fields dropped. A server is to pass a program the key modifiers, the mouse's dot and modifiers, and COMPOSE, an input method's preedit as it changes. Today a program is told of a pointer's gone only as the lost release of what it held. It hears no TOUCH event and no contact after the first, no COMPOSE and no RESIZE, and no modifier bits, dots or device of a pointer or a key, so Control-C reaches a program as c.

The pointer's moves. Each session's moves are to reach its program through one slot, the latest replacing any not yet taken, at most 60 a second, counting no input units and never acknowledged, so that a client sending more, or a program slow to read, never builds a queue of moves. Today moves count no input units and are not acknowledged, and there is no slot and no rate: every move a server reads goes to its program as an input line, through the queue of 4,096 lines.

10.8 From a program to the server

DOC-1970 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1473, REQ-1476, REQ-1477, REQ-1480

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1473 The program may send to any connections it names at any time; the server forwards to those connections, a sub-page or a sub-page list only where its page is selected, and keeps nothing (to be rechecked on livedevelopment).

typeFieldsWhat it does
replyconnection, request, magazines, selected, one sub-page or nullThe answer to connected, exactly once: the channel's index for this viewer, and the sub-page its selection shows, or the initial page's first. The page MUST be listed in magazines. A second one is refused with not_awaited.
pageconnections, request or null, page: one sub-pageIt reaches only those connections whose selection shows it: those that selected it, and those that selected its page with 3F7F, whose selection it becomes.
deniedconnection, request, status, 403, 404, 429 or 503Ends a selection with no page, at once. The viewer is sent DENIED, and the connection stays open.
subpagesconnections, request or null, page, version, 0 to 65,535, subpages: sub and title, at most 40 characters or null, each sub-code once, in ascending orderA page's list of sub-pages, with the answer to a page selection and whenever it changes. Sent to the connections named whose selection shows the page and that do not hold that version.
indexconnections, magazinesThe index changed
redirectconnection, request, and page and sub, with a query it keeps or none, or address, at most 8,000 bytesSends the viewer elsewhere, with no action from them. With a page, a selection, so the program is then sent selected for it. With an address, no selection changes, and the program is sent left when the viewer goes. Naming both or neither is refused.
soundkind, connections, and the kind's fieldsA sound, its data, or a command
cookieconnection, name, value, path, max_age, seconds, 0 removing it, null for the browser's session; same_site, Strict, Lax or None; secure, http_onlySets one of the program's cookies. Each of name and value is within 4,096 bytes. A name starting _ is the server's, and is refused.
author_logconnections, level, E, W or I; text, 1 to 200 bytes; page or nullA line for the developer console of the viewer's receiver
loglevel, error, warn, info or debug; event, lower case, digits and _, at most 64 characters; text, at most 1,000 charactersA line for the server's log
packetsconnections, packets: base64 of whole 42-byte packetsRaw packets, delivered as they are. Each MUST be of a kind the stream may carry, or the line is refused with invalid_packet.
descriptionpage, the page describe named, and public. With public true, also title, 1 to 120 characters, text, 1 to 16,000 characters on one line, and content, one sub-page of that page. With public false, nothing else.The answer to describe, and the only way a page becomes public on the web: see describing a page. A content of another page is refused with wrong_page, and one no describe awaits with not_awaited.

A field that may be null may also be left out, and so may a redirect's unused fields.

What reaches the viewer. Every packet is checked with the engine's one check of a stream. Sub-pages go as page frames, the index of reply and index as index records, subpages as SUBPAGES records, author_log as LOG records, cookie as a SET_COOKIE record, redirect as a REDIRECT record, denied as a DENIED record, the Link Extension as LINKS records, sounds as audio records, and packets as they are.

Magazines.magazines is at most 8 objects, in ascending magazine, 1 to 8, each with a name of at most 120 characters and pages: 1 to 253, 251 in magazine 1, ascending, each page in that magazine and never reserved, with a title and a section of at most 120 characters, sectionnull for none, and subpages, its count, 1 to 8,190. The index is page-level: sub-pages are never listed in it.

Rests on: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 7.1.2 Packet address; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause A.10 Reserved page number; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.1 Packets X/27/0 to X/27/3 for Editorial Linking

10.9 A sub-page

DOC-1971 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1064, REQ-1346, REQ-1478, REQ-1501

A sub-page is the object a page and a reply carry, and every field but cursor is required.

FieldHolds
page, subIts page, never a reserved number, and its sub-code, 0000 to 3F7E
rows25 entries, rows 0 to 24, each exact base64 of 40 seven-bit character codes, or null for a blank row. Row 0's columns 8 to 39 are the program's header text, and columns 0 to 7 are the receiver's.
linksThe six X/27/0 links: red, green, yellow, cyan, the fifth and the index, each page and sub, or null
display_row_24X/27/0's flag
link_extensionkeys: each key, red, green, yellow, cyan or index, at most once, with a target and a label of at most 9 characters, 8 for another service, null for the Index key. numbers: at most 8, a number on the page and a target, never web:. texts: at most 8, row 1 to 23, column 0 to 39, length keeping the run within 40 columns, and a target. A target is as the Link Extension says.
controlC4 to C11 as erase, newsflash, subtitle, suppress_header, update, interrupted_sequence, inhibit_display and magazine_serial. The server sets C4 and C11 itself.
character_setregion, 0 to 15, carried in X/28/0, or null; national_option, 0 to 7, C12 to C14
enhancementsAt most 16 packets X/26, each designation 0 to 15 once, and 13 triplets of address, 6 bits, mode, 5 bits, and data, 7 bits
packetsAt most 16 other packed lines by packet: x27_nibbles, designation 1 to 3, with 39 nibbles of 4 bits; x27, designation 4 to 15; x28 and m29, designation 0 to 15, each of 13 triplets
cursorrow, column and block, or null

Examples of the forms: "links":[{"page":"101","sub":"3F7F"},null,null,null,null,null]; "link_extension":{"keys":[{"key":"red","target":"101","label":"NEXT"}],"numbers":[{"number":"101","target":"name:100"}],"texts":[{"row":1,"column":0,"length":4,"target":"101"}]}; "enhancements":[{"designation":0,"triplets":[{"address":41,"mode":0,"data":4}]}], with 13 triplets in all; and an entry of packets names its form in a field packet, such as {"packet":"x27_nibbles",...}.

The server encodes the sub-page with the engine's encoder and sends it alone. A sub-page's links go with it every time it is sent.

A cursor is on rows 1 to 23 and columns 0 to 39, as the cursor record holds no other: row 0 is the header and row 24 the Fastext row. Any other is refused with the rule out_of_range, so the whole line is refused, and input to the page is still acknowledged with a cursor record of no cursor.

Rests on: EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1 Page header; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.2 Page sub-code; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.3 Control bits; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.3.1.4 Data bytes; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 9.6.1 Packets X/27/0 to X/27/3 for Editorial Linking; EN 300 706 V1.2.1 clause 12.3 Non-spacing attributes and additional characters

10.10 Sounds

DOC-1972 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1060, REQ-1061, REQ-1062, REQ-1476

A sound message has a kind, and each has connections. The server turns it into the audio profile's records.

kindFields
opushandle 0 to 255, generation, packet_bytes 1 to 40, samples, packets: base64 of the packets back to back, at most two minutes. Every packet is checked with the engine's decoder against the profile.
opus_livehandle, generation, packet_bytes
opus_packetshandle, generation, packet_bytes, first, packets
objecthandle, generation, rate, 8000, 11025, 16000, 22050, 24000, 32000, 44100 or 48000, pcm: base64 of 16-bit little-endian mono samples, at most two minutes, encoded by the server as IMA ADPCM
livehandle, generation, rate
blockshandle, generation, rate, first, pcm
playchannel 0 to 3, handle, generation, gain 0 to 100, fade_ms, loop, after, duck 0 to 6, page or null
stopchannel, fade_ms
gainchannel, gain, fade_ms

10.11 Deadlines

DOC-1973 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1040, REQ-1471, REQ-1481

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1481 Every selection has an ending: when a program does not answer in time, or is not running, the viewer is told the channel is unavailable and the connection stays open (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

NameTimeWhat it bounds
PROGRAM_REPLY_LIMIT_MS, budgets.reply_limit_ms5,000 msHow long the server waits for an answer to connected or selected before it sends the viewer DENIED 503. It is provisional, and not yet measured.
FIRST_SELECTION_WAIT_MS1,500 msHow long connected is held for a connection's first SELECT
The session's grace5 minutesHow long a session waits for its viewer before closed
stats10 secondsHow often the figures are sent
Restarts1 to 30 secondsThe pause before a failing program is started again
ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_WAIT_MS250 msHow long an input count waits for a page frame to carry it before its cursor record goes alone. It is provisional, and not measured.
description_limit_ms2,000 msHow long a web visitor waits for a program's description. A later answer stands for those who come after, once.

A selection not yet answered keeps its deadline when its program ends, so its viewer is told the channel is unavailable. A program that started again is told of each connection by connected. A program that has no page yet answers reply with selectednull, and a page, redirect or denied after it.

10.12 What the kits give a program

DOC-1974 · concept · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1471, REQ-1674

The TypeScript kit, neottx-server-ts-protocol, and the Python kit, neoteletext_app, wrap this protocol, and copy the request number, so that a program built on them never handles it. The TypeScript kit keeps a screen for each page, and sends the rows as a whole sub-page when it is told to refresh.

A kit gives each selection to a page's application as a request object, in the shape a web framework gives a request, before the page opens. It is called for every selection, so that its path is never stale.

KeyHolds
method"GET": a selection only ever reads a page, and a viewer's keys are not requests
pathThe place selected, as an address names it: 302 or 302/0002
queryThe query, by name. Of a name given twice, the last.
queryTextThe query as written, without its ?, undecoded
cookiesThe viewer's cookies, by name, as their connection opened with them

All of it but method is untrusted, from the viewer. On a page that keeps a copy for each viewer, the UI library makes the page from the address's query, as it does from a redirect's params: ?article=5 reaches root(params).

10.13 Describing a page to the web

DOC-1975 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1728

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1728 A page is public on the web only when its program says so: a public page shows a person its title and a picture, with its text in the page, hidden and marked by the paywall structured data (to be rechecked on main).

A page is public on the web only when its channel's program says so, page by page, and by default it is not. The server asks, and the program's answer is the only way a page becomes public. The answer goes to everyone, so a program builds it from no viewer. The Cloudflare host asks nothing yet, and answers every page as not public.

typeFieldsWhat it does
describepage, and nothing else: no connection, session, cookie or queryFrom the server, when a web visitor or a search engine is at a page's address. A reserved page is not asked about.
descriptionpage, the page describe named, and public. With public true, also title, 1 to 120 characters, text, 1 to 16,000 characters on one line, and content, one sub-page of that page. With public false, nothing else.From the program, the answer. A content of another page is refused with wrong_page.

Not public. A page the program says is not public, a page that does not exist, a reserved page, and a page the program did not answer for within description_limit_ms, 2,000 ms, all give one answer: 200, the small page and noindex. It differs only in the address its link repeats, so an address says nothing of whether a private page exists.

Public. The page's title; a picture of the page, which the server draws from content and carries inline as a PNG data: address, left out over 64 KiB; the offer to open it or get NeoTeletext; a canonical link to <channel>NNN; and the text in <div class="paywall">, which the page's stylesheet hides. JSON-LD declares that element with isAccessibleForFree false, the structured data Google documents for paywalled content. A sub-page's address and an address with a query show the page's description. Everyone gets the same bytes: no request header reaches the answer, so everything a page marked public gives is readable by anyone.

A program's words. A title or text is escaped for HTML and for the JSON-LD block, so it can never be markup, and the Content-Security-Policy allows the inline image and one fixed stylesheet by its hash, and no script.

Late and unasked. An answer after description_limit_ms reaches nobody who waited, and is taken once, for those who come after, while the not-public answer the wait left stands. A second answer, or one nobody asked for, is refused with not_awaited, so a program cannot fill the server's cache.

Bounds. An answer is kept for a minute, not-public answers and unanswered pages included, one cache for all visitors. A program is asked for at most 20 pages a second, 40 at once after a quiet time, and what is over that is answered as not public and not kept. At most 1,024 visitors wait, and 256 answers are kept. All of these are provisional.

11 Running a server

DOC-2026 · section

11.1 The administration listener

DOC-2030 · reference · normative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1052, REQ-1054, REQ-1055

The NeoTeletext server answers an operator on an address of its own, given by --admin-listen ADDRESS. With no such option there is none. It is never the address viewers reach, because figures about viewers stay off the public address, and nothing on it needs a client's or a program's author. A deployment puts it on 127.0.0.1:9464. The Cloudflare host has no such listener.

PathAnswer
/metrics200, text/plain; version=0.0.4; charset=utf-8: the figures since the process started, in the Prometheus text exposition format, version 0.0.4
/stats.json200, application/json: an object with type, server and channels, each channel by its folder, holding what a program is sent in stats, with invalid_packets, stale_input and stale_answers besides, and all_time: the counters and peaks since the server first ran with its state directory
/healthz200, ok: the process is running
/readyz200, ready, once every channel's program has answered since it started, and otherwise 503, no program answering: and the channels' folders. A program that has started may still be loading, so starting does not count: the server asks each program one describe as it starts, and any answer counts.
Anything else404

Every answer has Cache-Control: no-store. The method is not looked at. /stats.json on the viewers' address answers 404, as anything outside what each address answers does.

Metrics. Names follow Prometheus's conventions: the prefix teletext_, base units, and _total on every counter. Nothing identifies a viewer.

KindNames
Of the processteletext_build_info, with the label version; process_start_time_seconds; process_resident_memory_bytes
Gauges of the serverteletext_connections, teletext_connections_peak
Counters of the serverteletext_connections_total, teletext_messages_sent_total, teletext_sent_bytes_total, teletext_http_requests_total, teletext_program_restarts_total
Gauges of a channel, with the label channelteletext_channel_viewers, teletext_channel_viewers_peak, teletext_channel_sessions_waiting, teletext_channel_ready
Counters of a channelteletext_channel_viewers_total, teletext_channel_sessions_resumed_total, teletext_channel_page_opens_total, teletext_channel_inputs_total, teletext_channel_keys_total, teletext_channel_pages_sent_total, teletext_channel_refused_total, teletext_channel_stale_input_total, teletext_channel_stale_answers_total, teletext_channel_redirects_total, teletext_channel_sound_records_total, teletext_channel_program_lines_sent_total, teletext_channel_program_lines_received_total, teletext_channel_program_lines_dropped_total, teletext_channel_events_total
A histogram of a channelteletext_channel_event_duration_seconds: the channel's time over one event and its effects

11.2 The server's options and what it keeps

DOC-2031 · reference · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1041, REQ-1054

teletext-server is the native server. The channels file says what it serves.

OptionHoldsDefault
--listen ADDRESSWhere viewers connect. A listening socket systemd passes, by the protocol of sd_listen_fds(3), is taken in its place, so that a connection made during a restart waits in the socket. More than one such socket is refused.0.0.0.0:8090
--origin URLThe origin viewers reach the server at, which each channel's address is on. An https origin marks its cookies Secure.http://127.0.0.1:8090
--channels FILEThe channels fileNone
--state DIRECTORYWhere it keeps what follows, across restarts$STATE_DIRECTORY, as systemd sets it, or nothing kept
--admin-listen ADDRESSThe administration listenerNone
--log-format auto|json|textHow events are written to standard error. RUST_LOG chooses which.auto

What it keeps. No channel's content. A file in the state directory is written beside its place and renamed into it, so a crash leaves the old file or the new one, and the files and their folder are their owner's alone.

FileHolds
session.keyThe key that signs the session cookie, made when there is none
session.key.previousThe key before it, if an operator keeps one. A cookie that verifies under either stands, so a key is changed by moving session.key here and starting the server, and no viewer loses a session.
figures.jsonThe totals behind all_time, written every minute and at shutdown. A file that does not read is kept aside as figures.json.unreadable, and the figures start again.

Stopping. On SIGTERM or SIGINT the server closes each connection with 4100, so that receivers come back, keeps its figures, and exits, within 10 seconds. A program keeps its own state, and is told of each connection when it starts or connects again.

12 Tasks

DOC-1788 · section

12.1 Build a static channel

DOC-1873 · task · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-91, REQ-93, REQ-508, REQ-513

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-91 The page checker (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-93 Writing a channel's contents (not implemented on livedevelopment); REQ-508 The files of a channel (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-513 `channel.ttx`, a channel's entry file: its settings, as JSON (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

This builds a channel any web server can host, with no program. It needs a folder, a text editor and neottx.

  1. Make a folder. Its name is the last part of the channel's address, unless it is the root of a site.
  2. Write each page as NNN.tti, its name the page number. A page needs a PN line and OL lines, as in TTI page files. Give it a DE line to title it, and start each sub-page of a page with a new PN and SC.
  3. Add links with FL, or the Link Extension's lines.
  4. Write channel.ttx, for example { "name": "Example", "initial_page": "100" }. Leave out push. The file is optional: with none, the channel opens at page 100.
  5. Run neottx check 100.tti 101.tti, with --descriptor channel.ttx when links name other services, and correct what it reports. A row over 40 cells is an error, among others, and it exits with status 4 on an error.
  6. Run neottx contents . > contents.txt in the folder. This lists every page by its title. Add x lines by hand for other channels.
  7. Upload the folder to a web server. Check that a missing file answers 404, not an HTML page with status 200.
  8. Run neottx probe https://example.org/news/. It opens the channel as a receiver does, says what each step found, and exits non-zero on a fault. For a server on this machine, add --allow-local 127.0.0.1.
  9. Open the channel in NeoTeletext at the same address.

A channel of many pages opens faster with a bundle: neottx convert 100.tti --to t42 -o index.t42 writes one page's packets, and a bundle may hold any number of them, up to 256 KiB.

12.2 Build a client

DOC-1976 · task · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-494, REQ-1732

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1732 One single published protocol document defines NeoTeletext's entire protocol: upstream, downstream, teletext rendering, audio and everything else (not implemented on main).

This builds a receiver that opens a live channel and shows a page. It needs a WebSocket, or a TCP socket to write one on, and the standard's decoding of a packet. Each step was tried with 150 lines of Python and nothing but its standard library, against the NeoTeletext server.

  1. Fetch channel.ttx from the channel's folder. Read it as the settings. With no push member the channel is static: read its files as opening a channel says, and stop here. With one, note initial_page, and open nothing else over HTTP.
  2. Open a WebSocket at the folder's push, with Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: neottx.7 and no Origin, as opening the connection says. Check that the 101 echoes the name. A browser's WebSocket always sends Origin, so it cannot do this.
  3. Send HELLO as one binary message with the capability SELECT: FF 09 07 80 80 80 02, which is version 7 and capabilities 4,194,304. Add 131,072 if you will draw a cursor. Cursor records come either way: they carry the acknowledgement, so skip them if you draw none. Mask it, as a client's WebSocket frames always are.
  4. Read binary messages. Cut each into 42-byte packets, and discard and count a message that is not whole packets.
  5. Read each packet's two address bytes as Hamming 8/4. Bytes that decode to 12 and 15 are data channel 12: check the CRC-16, gather the fragments by record id and index, and when the last has come, check the frame's CRC-32 and read the type at byte 1. Skip a type you do not read.
  6. Wait for WELCOME, type 5, and check its version. Give up after 10 seconds.
  7. Send RESIZE, FOCUS_OUT and SELECT of the initial page, in one message if you like: FF 07 28 19 00 00 FF 06 FF 40 03 31 30 30 selects page 100. If keys go to this channel, send FOCUS_IN after the selection, because a program hears focus only from then.
  8. Decode the packets that are not data channel 12 as teletext packets: a header starts a page and erases it when C4 is set, and each row packet replaces its row. Page 100, sub-code 0000, whole, has the header 02 15 15 15 15 D0 15 15 15 02: the two address bytes, then Hamming 8/4 digits, in which byte 5 is S2 + 8 × C4 and byte 9 holds C11 in its lowest bit. Clear the row's parity bit to read its characters. They are not ASCII: the page's national option chooses them, and codes below 0x20 are attributes that show as spaces. Columns 0 to 7 of row 0 are yours to fill, with the page number. Show row 24 only when X/27/0 says to.
  9. Read DENIED, type 12, as the end of a selection that has no page, and say so to the viewer. Do not set a timer of your own: the server ends every selection.
  10. Answer each ping with a pong, and ping a connection silent for 25 seconds.
  11. Send each key as KEY, under the stamp of the page and sub-code on screen, which are the header's, never 3F7F.
  12. Send SELECT whenever the page shown changes. Read each index and SUBPAGES record if you show lists, and REDIRECT always, because a channel moves a viewer with it.
  13. When the connection closes, stop or try again as its close code says. On trying again, begin at step 2, and select the page you were showing.
  14. Run the wire vectors through your reader and writer.
  15. Try it against a real server. teletext-server is built from neoteletext-app-server, and neottx from neoteletext-engine, each with cargo build.

12.3 Build a server

DOC-1977 · task · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1732, REQ-1882

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1732 One single published protocol document defines NeoTeletext's entire protocol: upstream, downstream, teletext rendering, audio and everything else (not implemented on main).

This builds a server for one live channel, which any receiver can open. Each step was tried with 200 lines of Python and nothing but its standard library, and checked with neottx probe.

  1. Answer GET <channel>channel.ttx with 200 and application/json: {"name":"Example","initial_page":"100","push":{"input":true}}.
  2. On a GET of <channel>push, refuse an Origin with 403, and an offer without neottx.7 with 426, naming it, as opening the connection says. Otherwise answer 101, with Sec-WebSocket-Accept, and Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: neottx.7. Do not offer compression.
  3. Answer every other path as what each address answers says, or 404.
  4. Read each binary message whole, unmasking it, and read it as the input stream. A message cut short, an unknown core type, a flag nobody defined or malformed text is a protocol error: send a close of 4000. Skip an extension you do not know by its length, and ignore text messages.
  5. On HELLO, with any version but 7, close with 4001. Otherwise answer with WELCOME: a record of type 5 whose body is FF 01, the version as uv, and the capabilities it will use, a subset of those offered, as uv.
  6. Write a record as the record frame says: version 2, the type, the body's length, the body, and the CRC-32 of all that, big-endian.
  7. Carry the record as packets of data channel 12: cut it into 34-byte fragments, the last padded with zeros. Each packet is the Hamming 8/4 bytes of 12, 15, service byte 8 and the control byte, with the value 1 for a first fragment and 2 for a last, which are bits 1 and 2 as EN 300 706 counts them; then the record id, the index, the 34 bytes, and the CRC-16 of those 36 bytes, whose polynomial is x16 + x9 + x7 + x4 + 1. Number each record one on from the last, from 0 at WELCOME, modulo 256.
  8. On SELECT, read the place as the map reads it. A SELECT that repeats a selection that stands does nothing, and a page answered with a sub-page has become that sub-page, so the page's own SELECT is new, and one that repeats a selection you ended with DENIED is a new selection. An empty one ends the selection.
  9. Answer every selection within 5,000 ms, with a page frame: a header with C4 and C11 set, such as 02 15 15 15 15 D0 15 15 15 02 for page 100, sub-code 0000, and the page's rows, as one binary message. A header's bytes are Hamming 8/4 and its 32 display bytes, like a row's 40, have odd parity. Or answer with DENIED.
  10. Send a changed sub-page as a header with C4 clear and the rows that changed, for the same page and sub-code only.
  11. Keep what each connection holds, and on FOCUS_OUT, a change of selection or a lost connection, treat it as released, as held input says.
  12. Answer pings, and send a close of 4100 before a restart, so that receivers come back.
  13. Run the third part of the conformance table, what a server answers, and the wire vectors against it.
  14. teletext-server is built from neoteletext-app-server, and neottx from neoteletext-engine, each with cargo build. Run neottx probe http://127.0.0.1:8090/example/ --allow-local 127.0.0.1. It opens the channel through the same process as a viewer's receiver, selects the initial page and page 8FC, and ends with result no fault.

12.4 Build a program

DOC-1978 · task · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1476, REQ-1732, REQ-1882

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1732 One single published protocol document defines NeoTeletext's entire protocol: upstream, downstream, teletext rendering, audio and everything else (not implemented on main).

This builds a channel behind the NeoTeletext server, in any language, with nothing but a JSON library. Each step was tried with a program of 60 lines of Python.

  1. Make a folder, and write a program there that reads lines from standard input and writes one JSON object a line to standard output, flushing each.
  2. Write a channels file, as processes, transports and lines says, naming the program's command and directory.
  3. teletext-server is built from neoteletext-app-server, and neottx from neoteletext-engine, each with cargo build. Start the server: teletext-server --listen 127.0.0.1:8090 --origin http://127.0.0.1:8090 --channels channels.json --state state. The first line your program reads is start. Ignore a type you do not use, such as stats, every 10 seconds, and never stop on one.
  4. Answer describe, which comes second, with {"type":"description","page":"100","public":false}. Until you do, the channel does not count as ready.
  5. Make a sub-page. Every field is required but cursor. A row is base64 of 40 character codes, and a blank row is null: {"page":"100","sub":"0000","rows":[...],"links":[null,null,null,null,null,null],"display_row_24":false,"link_extension":{"keys":[],"numbers":[],"texts":[]},"control":{"erase":false,"newsflash":false,"subtitle":false,"suppress_header":false,"update":false,"interrupted_sequence":false,"inhibit_display":false,"magazine_serial":false},"character_set":{"region":null,"national_option":0},"enhancements":[],"packets":[],"cursor":null}.
  6. Make the index: [{"magazine":1,"name":"Example","pages":[{"page":"100","title":"Hello","section":null,"subpages":1}]}].
  7. On connected, read selection, which is null for the initial page, and answer with reply, copying connection and request, with the index and the sub-page as selected. A page that does not exist is selectednull, and a denied after it.
  8. On selected, answer {"type":"page","connections":[1],"request":2,"page":{...}} with the connection and number you were given, and keep each connection's latest number: redirect and denied copy it, and input gives you none, or {"type":"denied","connection":1,"request":2,"status":404}.
  9. For a page of several sub-pages, answer a selection whose sub is 3F7F with the first in ascending order, and send subpages with it. A selection of one sub-code gets that sub-page alone.
  10. When a page changes, send it again to the connections that show it, with requestnull. The server works out the changed rows.
  11. On input, act on the event, and send what changed. Move a viewer with redirect. On left and closed, let go of what you keep.
  12. Print each refused line to standard error, which reaches the server's log. Its path and rule name the first fault.
  13. Run neottx probe http://127.0.0.1:8090/example/ --allow-local 127.0.0.1 --select 101 --select '100?a=1'. Each selection reports a page or DENIED, with its time, and a page your program did not answer in time fails with DENIED 503.

13 Troubleshooting

DOC-1789 · section

13.1 A channel will not open, and channel.ttx looks right

DOC-1874 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-507, REQ-513, REQ-516

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-507 Channels: a channel is a folder (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-513 `channel.ttx`, a channel's entry file: its settings, as JSON (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-516 Media types and content checks of a channel's files (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Problem. A receiver reports a failure on opening a channel, not the channel's pages.

Causes.

  • A member of channel.ttx is unknown or duplicated, which refuses the whole file: a misspelled name, or a member that was withdrawn, such as push.address or push.method.
  • The file answers with type text/html. A host often gives this for a missing file with status 200.
  • The address has no / at the end of its folder, or breaks the canonical form.

Fix. Run neottx probe on the channel's address. It names the step that failed. A folder holding only an older channel.json is not a fault: that file is never asked for, and the channel opens with the defaults, so rename it.

13.2 A page shows "not found", or a page is a failure

DOC-1875 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-313, REQ-508, REQ-516

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-313 Pages a viewer can key (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-508 The files of a channel (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment); REQ-516 Media types and content checks of a channel's files (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Problem. A page in the folder does not show.

Causes and fixes.

  • The name. A page file is exactly three digits and an extension, with A to F in capitals. Rename 1fe.t42 to 1FE.t42.
  • A failure, not "not found". The file answered 200 and failed its check: a TTI file with no PN line, a T42 file that is not whole packets, or a type of text/html. Run neottx check, and see media types and checks.
  • Another page's file. A page file fills only the page asked for, so a file named 100.tti that holds page 200 shows nothing at 100. Its PN line must match the name.
  • A hexadecimal number. A viewer cannot key a page with a digit from A to F. Link to it.

13.3 The upgrade is answered 426

DOC-1979 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-494

Problem. The push upgrade is answered 426 Upgrade Required, with Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: neottx.7 when the request asked to upgrade, and with neither that nor Upgrade when it did not. A receiver reports the upgrade as refused, and does not try again.

Causes.

  • The request offers no Sec-WebSocket-Protocol, or offers another, such as neottx.6: the receiver is of another version than the server.
  • The request has no WebSocket upgrade at all: a browser or curl visited <channel>push.
  • The name differs in case. It is matched exactly.

Fix. Offer the name in the 426 response. There is no compatibility path, so receiver and server must speak one version: update the older. Run neottx probe on the channel's address, whose line handshake accepted: neottx.7 shows a good upgrade.

13.4 The upgrade is answered 403

DOC-1980 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-494

Problem. The push upgrade is answered 403 Forbidden, whatever the subprotocol.

Cause. The request carries Origin. A server refuses any push upgrade that does, whatever its value, null included. This is what stops a web page reaching a channel with a viewer's cookies.

Fix. Send no Origin header. A web page's WebSocket always sends one and cannot be told not to, so a receiver cannot be written in a web page. A WebSocket library for a native program often has an option that adds one: turn it off. A proxy in front of the server must not add one either.

13.5 The connection closes with 4000 or 4001, and does not come back

DOC-1981 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-201

Problem. A connection closes, and a receiver does not reconnect, which is right: a stop code says the fault would be there on the next attempt.

Causes of 4001. HELLO named a version other than 7.

Causes of 4000. The channel read something it could not. These are the usual ones.

  • The retired PAGE event, FF 0A, or any other unknown core type.
  • A SELECT in the old binary form, FF 40 04 80 02 FF 7E. Its payload is the place as text now: FF 40 03 31 30 30.
  • A SELECT with small hexadecimal digits, a sub-code of 3F7F, or a query that is not pairs.
  • An event cut by the end of its message. A message holds whole events and whole characters.
  • A varint longer than it need be, or a mods flag set with a value of 0.
  • A control character in raw text. Enter and the like are KEY events.
  • A WebSocket fault: an unmasked frame, or a message over 8,192 bytes.

Fix. Run the message through the wire vectors' rows, whose refused rows are these faults.

A close of 4100 is not a fault: the server is restarting, and a receiver comes back by itself.

13.6 Every selection ends with DENIED 503

DOC-1982 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1481, REQ-1882

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1481 Every selection has an ending: when a program does not answer in time, or is not running, the viewer is told the channel is unavailable and the connection stays open (tested, not recorded as met on livedevelopment).

Problem. A receiver reports that the channel's program is not answering, and neottx probe exits non-zero with DENIED 503: the channel's program did not answer.

Causes.

  • No program is running: the server's log says why it ended. DENIED then comes at once, not after 5 seconds.
  • The program answers selected but never answered connected, or the other way round. Both need an answer, and the server's log names which with the event program_late.
  • The answer does not flush. A line a program buffers does not reach the server.
  • The answer was refused. The program is sent refused, and nothing of the line is done: read the program's standard error or the server's log, event line_refused.
  • The answer carries an old request, and was dropped and counted as stale_answers. Copy the number from the message you are answering.
  • A reply with selectednull, and no page, redirect or denied after it.
  • The answer is a sub-page other than the one selected, which is not refused and goes nowhere. The log says only program_late.
  • A reply gives a page the index does not hold, which is refused with not_in_index.

Fix. Answer every connected and selected within 5,000 ms, with denied for a page that does not exist.

13.7 HELLO has no answer, or packets count as invalid

DOC-1983 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-210, REQ-1462

Unfinished. The project's records do not show all of this as met, so it may not hold in full: REQ-1462 Only packets of the allowed kinds downstream; a receiver discards, counts and reports the rest (tested, not recorded as met on main).

Problem. A receiver reports invalid packets on the push connection, or that the channel did not answer HELLO with WELCOME, on a server you wrote.

Causes.

  • The CRC-16 of a packet of data channel 12 is wrong. Its polynomial is that of EN 300 708 clause 6.5.8.1, x16 + x9 + x7 + x4 + 1, from a register of zeros, with no final inversion. It is not CRC-16-CCITT.
  • The record is not padded to 34 bytes, so the packet is not 42 bytes, and the whole message is discarded.
  • The Service Byte's bit 4 is clear, or the control byte's first and last bits are not set on a record of one fragment.
  • A message that is a text frame. A server sends binary alone.
  • The WELCOME sets a capability HELLO did not offer.

Fix. Compare your packet with the row welcome of the wire vectors, byte for byte.

13.8 Keys or clicks never reach the program

DOC-1984 · troubleshooting · informative · NeoTeletext's own: no standard defines it · requirements REQ-1555, REQ-1557

Problem. A channel shows its page, and a program hears no input, or hears only some.

Causes.

  • The stamp is not the selection. A press stamped with another page or sub-code is dropped, and stale_input in the server's figures counts it. A page selected with 302 becomes a sub-page when it arrives: stamp the sub-code on screen, which is the header's, never 3F7F.
  • No selection was made, so every stamped event is dropped.
  • The event is one a program does not hear: what a program hears of input says which.
  • A click on a link the page declares is the receiver's, and does not go to the channel.

Glossary

Active

What a selected page is to the back end: it receives the back end's updates, whatever the viewer's input is doing. A page can be active without being focused, for example in a tab behind another or with the pointer outside the page. It is not a word for a tab: a tab in front is "the tab in front" or "focused". The object kind "Active object" (TERM-1626) is a different thing, from EN 300 706. Source: DEC-1538 version 4, the owner's words of 30 October 2026.

TERM-1650

Active object

An object that behaves like extra local enhancement data of the page.

TERM-1626 · also active objects · broader term Object

Adaptive object

An object that adapts to the attributes under it.

TERM-1627 · also adaptive objects · broader term Object

Attribute

A change of colour or style on a teletext page: either a spacing attribute (a Level 1 control code that occupies a cell) or a non-spacing attribute (an X/26 triplet that uses no cell).

TERM-1602 · also attributes

Cell
