One character position in a row of a teletext page; a row has 40 cells. A spacing attribute occupies a cell, which normally shows as a space; a non-spacing attribute does not use one up.

TERM-1588 · also cells, character cell · broader term Row

Character set

One of the sets of characters a teletext page draws from: G0 (the main text set), G1 (block mosaics), G2 (supplementary) and G3 (Level 2.5's smoothed mosaics and line drawing). DRCS characters were downloadable but are removed from the project.

TERM-1612 · also character sets

CLUT

Colour Look-Up Table: one of the four tables of 8 colours that make up the Colour Map. CLUT 0 is the classic 8 teletext colours. CLUT 1 is their half-intensity versions, with entry 0 meaning transparent. CLUTs 2 and 3 are 16 further colours.

TERM-1620 · also CLUTs, Colour Look-Up Table · broader term Colour Map

Colour Map

The 32-colour palette of Levels 2.5 and 3.5, organised as four CLUTs of 8 colours each.

TERM-1619

Default object

An object a page gets automatically from its magazine's table, the MOT.

TERM-1629 · also default objects · broader term Object

DRCS

Dynamically Redefinable Character Set: characters whose pixel patterns are downloaded from special pages. The project removed DRCS on 20 October 2026 (decision E.95).

TERM-1618 · also Dynamically Redefinable Character Set · broader term Character set

Enhancement data

Data carried in X/26 packets as triplets, each a command such as "at row 5, column 10, put character é" or "from here, background colour 19". Objects are reusable blocks of it.

TERM-1594

Focused

What a selected page is for the viewer's input, and only for input. Keys reach the page only while it holds the keyboard focus; mouse, wheel, touch and hover reach it only while the pointer is over the page area. Losing the focus never changes the selection and never stops updates. Source: DEC-1538 version 4, the owner's words of 30 October 2026.

TERM-1651

G0

The main text character set: Latin with a national option, Cyrillic, Greek, Arabic or Hebrew.

TERM-1613 · broader term Character set

G1

The block-mosaic graphics character set.

TERM-1614 · broader term Character set

G2

The supplementary character set: accents and symbols.

TERM-1615 · broader term Character set

G3

Level 2.5's character set of smoothed mosaics and line-drawing shapes.

TERM-1616 · broader term Character set

GPOP

Global POP: a Public Object Page for the whole magazine.

TERM-1625 · also Global POP, Global Public Object Page · broader term Object page

Header

Row 0 of a teletext page, which carries the page number, the service name and the clock. The receiver's status area is part of it.

TERM-1589 · also header row, row 0 · broader term Row

Level 1

The basic teletext page: rows of characters with spacing attributes.

TERM-1608 · broader term Presentation Level

Level 1.5

Level 1 plus extra characters, such as accented letters, written by X/26.

TERM-1609 · broader term Presentation Level

Level 2.5

Level 1.5 plus non-spacing attributes, 32 colours (16 of them redefinable), side panels, objects and a few downloadable characters.

TERM-1610 · broader term Presentation Level

Level 3.5

Level 2.5 with all 32 colours redefinable, more downloadable characters, and bold, italic and proportional text.

TERM-1611 · broader term Presentation Level

M/29/0

Packet 29, designation 0, for a whole magazine: the settings X/28/0 carries for one page, applied to every page in the magazine.

TERM-1600 · broader term Packet

M/29/4

Packet 29, designation 4, for a whole magazine: the settings X/28/4 carries for one page, applied to every page in the magazine.

TERM-1601 · broader term Packet

Magazine

A group of teletext pages sharing their first digit: pages 100 to 199 are magazine 1. M/29 packets carry settings for every page in a magazine.

TERM-1592 · also magazines

MOT

Magazine Organization Table, page xFE: lists, for each page, which object pages and default objects it uses.

TERM-1630 · also Magazine Organization Table

National option

The variant of the Latin G0 set (English, German, French and so on), chosen by three bits, C12 to C14, in the page header.

TERM-1617 · also national options · broader term G0

Non-spacing attribute

An X/26 triplet that changes colour or style from a cell without using up that cell. It is the main feature of Level 2.5.

TERM-1606 · also non-spacing attributes · broader term Attribute

Object

A reusable block of enhancement data, kept on a separate object page. Objects are active, adaptive or passive; default objects come to a page automatically from its magazine's table (the MOT).

TERM-1622 · also objects · broader term Enhancement data

Object page

A page that holds objects rather than text: a POP (Public Object Page) or a GPOP (Global POP, for the whole magazine).

TERM-1623 · also object pages

Packet

A unit of 40 bytes in which a teletext page arrives, numbered. Packets 1 to 25 carry the rows' text. Packets 26 to 29 carry extra data that old receivers ignore. X/n names packet n of a page, where X is the magazine number; M/n names packet n for a whole magazine.

TERM-1591 · also packets

Page

A teletext page is 25 rows, numbered 0 to 24, of 40 character cells each. Row 0 is the header. A page arrives as numbered packets and belongs to a magazine.

TERM-1586 · also pages, teletext page

Passive object

An object that carries its own attributes.

TERM-1628 · also passive objects · broader term Object

PDC

Programme Delivery Control: video-recorder timing data that can also travel in X/26. It displays nothing.

TERM-1634 · also Programme Delivery Control

POP

Public Object Page: an object page whose objects the pages that link to it use.

TERM-1624 · also Public Object Page · broader term Object page

Presentation Level

One of the cumulative capability levels of a teletext receiver: Level 1, Level 1.5, Level 2.5 and Level 3.5. A Level 2.5 receiver also shows Level 1 and Level 1.5 pages.

TERM-1607 · also Presentation Levels

Row

One of the 25 lines of a teletext page, numbered 0 to 24, each of 40 character cells. Row 0 is the header; packets 1 to 25 carry the rows' text.

TERM-1587 · also rows · broader term Page

SAA5050

The Mullard/Philips Level 1 character generator chip in nearly every 1980s UK teletext set. Its behaviour is the project's rendering baseline (decisions E.12 and E.13).

TERM-1631

SAA5246A

A later Philips chip that decodes Level 1.5.

TERM-1632

Selected

The page and sub-page a tab shows, told to the back end with SELECT. A tab has at most one selection. A page selected is also active. "Selection" is the noun. Source: DEC-1538 version 4, the owner's words of 30 October 2026.

TERM-1649

Set-after

A spacing attribute that takes effect from the next cell, not its own.

TERM-1605 · also set after · broader term Spacing attribute

Set-at

A spacing attribute that takes effect in its own cell.

TERM-1604 · also set at · broader term Spacing attribute

Side panel

Up to 16 extra columns beside the 40-column page, at Level 2.5 and 3.5.

TERM-1621 · also side panels

Spacing attribute

A Level 1 control code, such as "red text", "flash" or "double height". It occupies a cell, which normally shows as a space. It is either set-at (it takes effect in its own cell) or set-after (it takes effect from the next cell).

TERM-1603 · also spacing attributes · broader term Attribute

Status area

The receiver's own part of the header: row 0, columns 0 to 7, where the receiver shows the page number being searched for.

TERM-1590 · broader term Header

Triplet

An 18-bit command of enhancement data, protected by Hamming 24/18 error correction. Each says, for example, "at row 5, column 10, put character é", or "from here, background colour 19".

TERM-1595 · also triplets · broader term Enhancement data

TTI

A common text file format for teletext pages.

TERM-1635

X/26

Packet 26 of a page (X is the magazine number). It carries the page's enhancement data, as triplets.

TERM-1593 · broader term Packet

X/27/4

Packet 27, designation 4, of a page: one of the two packets (with X/27/5) that carry a page's links to the pages that hold its objects.

TERM-1596 · broader term Packet

X/27/5

Packet 27, designation 5, of a page: one of the two packets (with X/27/4) that carry a page's links to the pages that hold its objects.

TERM-1597 · broader term Packet

X/28/0

Packet 28, designation 0, of a page: page-wide settings, namely character sets, the screen and row colours, the side panels and colour redefinitions (with X/28/4). M/29/0 carries the same settings for a whole magazine.

TERM-1598 · broader term Packet

X/28/4

Packet 28, designation 4, of a page: page-wide settings, namely character sets, the screen and row colours, the side panels and colour redefinitions (with X/28/0). M/29/4 carries the same settings for a whole magazine.

TERM-1599 · broader term Packet

Unfinished items

The project's records do not show these 135 requirements as implemented, so what the items say of them may not hold in full. Each is listed once, with every item that rests on it. “To be rechecked” means it was met and has been revised since. “Tested, not recorded as met” means tests check it and nobody has yet recorded the whole of it as met. “Not implemented” means nothing records it as met and no test checks it: a passage beginning Not built says so where that is known. An item says in its own text what is not built.

Verified by inspection

No test can establish these requirements. Each was read, for the reason given.

  • REQ-1045 One protocol on every transport: Read at neoteletext-app-server 3572de8: `crates/teletext-native/src/apps.rs` has one `relay`, generic over its reader and writer, called for standard input and output and for the Unix socket, and `Channel::from_program` is told nothing of the transport. No test can check this: a test runs one relay and shows nothing about whether a second could be written.
  • REQ-1359 Privacy stated: Read on 2 November 2026: DOC-1946's paragraph on what a channel learns and DOC-2033's row on the channel tracking the pointer, and its Privacy paragraph, say that a channel learns the pointer's position over its own open page while keys go to it, and nothing elsewhere: not over another tab, the receiver's own controls or another window. No test can check this, because the requirement is that the document says it.
  • REQ-299 World System Teletext numbering only: Read at engine 6112177: `crates/neoteletext-core/src/limits.rs` is the one definition of magazines, page numbers and sub-codes, in EN 300 706's layout alone, and `packet/header.rs` decodes only that form. No test can show that a thing is absent: the requirement forbids the addressing of other systems, and it is checked by reading that none is there.
  • REQ-357 Bits and bytes that the standard marks as reserved are ignored when decoding: Read at engine 6112177: reserved data has no effect in `packet/editorial_links.rs` (only data bit 4 of the link control byte), `packet/triplet_fields.rs` (D3 of Display Attributes), `packet/function.rs` and `level15.rs` (data below 2/0). No test can cover this, because the requirement is universal over every reserved bit of the standard, and `every_mode_has_its_fields` and `magazine_packets` check only two of them.
  • REQ-373 The cell grid as the primary output: Read at engine 6112177: `cell_line`, `render_row`, `render_line` and `render_screen` in `crates/neoteletext-core/src/render/` take a grid and no packet, and the other renderers are built on them. Its tests build grids by hand, which shows a renderer needs nothing but the grid, and no test can show that no renderer decodes packets itself.
  • REQ-70 Internal character representation: Read at engine 6112177: `CharRef` in `crates/neoteletext-core/src/charset.rs` holds a `CharSet` (G0, G2 or G3), a 7-bit code and the diacritical mark's number, and `Glyph` in `level1/cell.rs` holds G1 as its 6-bit pattern, so a cell holds no Unicode value; `to_unicode` is called at output and to choose a joined form. No test can check this, because the requirement is about how the decoder holds characters and any representation could pass a test of what it draws.
